Digi Power X (DGXX) Q3 2026 Earnings Call Transcript

📅 Nov 13, 2026  |  Category: Technology  |  Read Source →

AI now drives nearly half of revenue as the Columbiana facility nears launch.

Need a quote from a Motley Fool analyst? Email [email protected]

Digi Power X Inc. (DGXX +3.17%) reported a transition toward AI infrastructure operations, as AI-related services now represent nearly half of the total revenue mix. Management stated that the company is focusing on completing its Columbiana facility and scaling the NeoCloudz platform to capture increasing demand for inference processing. The company confirmed that Phase I tenant access is scheduled for December 15, 2026, while the forward capital plan prioritizes growth through customer prepayments and project-level debt to minimize equity dilution. Management reported that the scarcity of permitted power assets is increasing the value of its current infrastructure portfolio.

Moderator: Good morning, and welcome to the Digi Power X Inc.'s Third Quarter 2026 Financial Results Conference Call. Please note that this event is being recorded, and a transcript will be available on Digi Power X Inc.'s website. At this time, all participants are in a listen-only mode. A brief question-and-answer session will follow the formal presentation. Unless noted otherwise, all amounts referred to during this call are denominated in U.S. dollars. Certain comments made during this call may include forward-looking statements or forward-looking information within the meaning of applicable U.S. and Canadian securities laws, including statements regarding the company's 2027 outlook, capital plan, and anticipated financing.

Such statements and information reflect current expectations and are, as such, are subject to a variety of risks and uncertainties that could cause actual results to differ materially from current expectations. Those risks and uncertainties include, but are not limited to, factors discussed in Digi Power X Inc.'s report on Form 10-Q for the three and nine months ended September 30th, 2026, and the annual report for the year ended December 31st, 2025, as well as the company's other disclosure documents. Except to the extent required by applicable law, Digi Power X undertakes no obligation to publicly update or review any forward-looking statements.

During the call, management may make reference to certain Non-GAAP financial measures that are not separately defined under GAAP, such as EBITDA and adjusted EBITDA. Management believes that those Non-GAAP measures, when considered in conjunction with GAAP financial measures, provide useful information for both management and investors. Reconciliations between GAAP and Non-GAAP measures are presented in tables accompanying the press release highlighting Digi Power X financial results for the quarter ended September 30th, 2026, which has been filed and made accessible under the company's continuous disclosure profile on SEDAR+ at www.sedarplus.ca, and is also available on the SEC's EDGAR website at www.sec.gov/edgar. I'd now like to turn the call over to Michel Amar, CEO of Digi Power X.

Michel Amar: Good morning, everyone, and thank you for joining us today as we share our results for the third quarter of 2026 for Digi Power X. Our third quarter results show that Digi Power X transformation into AI infrastructure company is beginning to take hold. Third quarter 2026 highlights. Our revenue went up 85% to $12.25 million from the second quarter of 2026, and up 50% from the third quarter of 2025. AI revenue of $5.66 million, up more than 400% from the second quarter, and representing now 46% of total revenue, compared with no AI revenue in the third quarter of 2025.

GPU rental bare metal through NeoCloudz of $2.46 million, up 127% from the second quarter, compared with no GPU rental revenue in the third quarter of 2025. Energy sales through our power plant of $3.29 million, up 68% from the second quarter, and 9% year-over-year. Legacy Bitcoin activities fell to approximately 27% of revenue from 63% a year ago. AI revenue more than offset that decline. EBITDA of positive EBITDA of $6.5 million and positive adjusted EBITDA of $600,000 compared with $1.7 million and $-1 million respectively in the third quarter of 2025. Positive net income of $1 million, materially supported by digital currency and warrant valuation gains, compared with net income of $100,000 in the third quarter of 2025.

Let's talk about the balance sheet and liquidity as of September 30, 2026. Cash and cash equivalents and digital currencies of $119.6 million. Working capital of $103 million, compared with $17.5 million at September 30, 2025. Digital currency holding of $24.5 million, up 66% year-over-year. Net fixed assets of $145.6 million, up 646% year-over-year, reflecting infrastructure investment primarily at the Columbiana, Alabama facility. No outstanding borrowing as of today. Balance sheet and liquidity as of today, November 13. The company's cash equivalents is approximately a little bit above $100 million cash. $168 million year-to-date capital expenditure deployed towards GPU equipment and data center build-out at the Columbiana, Alabama facility. Operational highlights and outlook. Columbiana, Alabama AI campus.

Construction of phase I remain on schedule. We expect to give our tenant access to the phase I facility on December 15, 2026. Basically, in 4.5 weeks from now. We'll start conditioning all the liquid cooling and power system at that time. With phase I billing expected to begin in January 2027. Work on phase II has started already for a few months, and subject to various conditions, we are targeting tenant access for phase II in March 2027. We are on schedule to be also on time to give access. A lot of the investment has been towards phase II already.

Our 10-year take-or-pay colocation agreement with our tenant, Cerebras, our AI colocation tenant at Columbiana is expected to provide phase I fees averaging approximately $40 million per year over the 10-year term, or approximately $402 million in total. Phase II would add approximately $58.5 million in annual fees, bringing combined average annual fees to approximately $107 million per year, and a total contract value to approximately $1.07 billion for both phases. The agreement also includes an option to extend the relationship for an additional term, which could increase the total potential contract value to approximately $2.5 billion. NeoCloudz Token Factory is live.

We launched this quarter from Berlin, our Token Factory, taking Digi Power X from AI infrastructure provider to direct seller of AI tokens. Running on our own power, data centers, and GPUs, the platform sells AI inference straight to customers. The huge advantage of that are the margins. We see margins anywhere from 85% to 90%, actual margins. So it's a business that will create a lot of positive cash flow as we invest in it. Because we own the full stack, we keep the value of every token we serve instead of renting out compute at wholesale prices.

We expect the Token Factory to unlock a new fast-growing revenue stream with meaningful higher margins, a major step in turning our infrastructure into full-scale AI business. Let's talk about the $200 million project-level debt financing. As announced on October 13, 2026, we received a commitment letter from a major lending institution for $200 million in debt financing, syndicated by Goldman Sachs to support the development of our 40 MW AI data center in Columbiana, Alabama, and to strengthen our balance sheet. The financing is expected to be structured at the project special purpose vehicle level, with an unsecured company guarantee through project completion and other limited matters.

The proceeds are intended to support completion of the Columbiana project and related project costs. Closing is currently expected to close in the fourth quarter of 2026, subject to related documentation and the satisfaction of various conditions, including the lender's completion of its due diligence. 2027 outlook and capital plan. We expect to reach an annualized revenue run rate of approximately $250 million-$300 million, or approximately $21 million-$25 million per month by the end of third quarter 2027, subject to adequate financing, market condition, and various other conditions. Approximately $143 million of that run rate is supported by current operation and the Columbiana tenant agreement.

The balance is expected to come from additional AI colocation and GPU capacity currently in development and in customer negotiations. CapEx. We expect 2027 CapEx of approximately $350 million. Primarily for approximately 10 MW of AI inference or Token Factory at 85%-90% margins, capacity and the completion of our phase II Columbiana. We intend to fund this principally through customer deposits and prepayment and contract debt financing, including the proposed $200 million facility together with cash on hand. This approach ties capital deployment to contracted revenue and is designed to minimize shareholder dilution. I want to remind every shareholder that in 2016, I invested from my personal cash $8 million, and I never sold one share.

Just to tell you that I have a long-term vision of a huge return on my investment. US Data Centers update. Digi Power X owns approximately 48% of US Data Centers Inc., which design and deploys the ARMS modular AI data center system and is developing URP-1, a robotic platform for automated deployment and maintenance. USDC is raising capital independently, which will further dilute our ownership interest without capital contribution from Digi Power X, without the issuance of Digi Power X shares. In order to fund our growth, which is very promising on US Data Center, we're going to raise capital without diluting Digi Power X or without getting any allocation on Digi Power X.

I think that's very important for everyone to understand that Digi Power X today is very focused on power, on GPU bare metal, on colocation deal, and cannot afford to allocate extra dollars to expand robotics or modular systems. That's the reason why we have a different setup to raise capital. What are the opportunity for growth and expansion? We continue to prepare our New York infrastructure assets for conversion to AI data center operation, and we are targeting the transition of these sites beginning of third quarter and fourth quarter 2027. These sites are powered.

One is powered for the last 10 years, 18.7 MW, and another one where we have a total of 120 MW, including our generation of power, is being powered for 30 years in terms of generation. We got an allocation from the utility, which is great because it's all green power, hydropower, and we got an allocation of 63 MW there. Because of our financial discipline, we haven't rushed to develop it simultaneously with the Columbiana, Alabama AI Center in order to control our CapEx. We don't want to rush and be out of cash.

As we transition from a project to execution, which we have been sharing videos of our progress, and I was there actually a few days ago, and it's really, we are very close to finalize phase I, few weeks. As we transition from project to execution to live, then we can keep on going with our other assets. In North Carolina, we own now two sites, about 40 acres adjacent to one of the state's largest electrical switchyard. Our current development plans contemplate 2x 75 MW, about 150 MW for 2029 and 2030. That will secure our growth for the future years.

Between New York and North Carolina, we can expand to over 400 MW worth of capacity, which depending how we optimize each megawatt, is huge revenues. We definitely have currently, we own the assets to grow 2x, 3x every year for the next few years. Revenue increased 85% over the previous quarter, and AI now accounts for nearly half of our business, all before the expected start of Columbiana colocation billing in January 2027. Our priority now is execution, financial discipline, and then we expand our compute capacity and convert our power assets into long-term contracted revenues. We ended our quarter with approximately $120 million in cash and cash equivalents that give us a strong foundation for this next phase.

We are building this business with a clear focus on value per share. Our approach is to secure bankable customer commitments, deploy capital against attractive returns, and fund expansion through customer contribution and contract-backed financing. Our ambition is substantial, and our discipline is equally clear: grow recurring revenue and cash generation while minimizing shareholder dilution. Thank you for joining us today. We will now take questions.

Moderator: Thank you. Our first question is, what do Q3 results demonstrate about Digi Power X's AI transformation?

Michel Amar: It is really an incredible quarter for us. If you think that last year we were a basically dying Bitcoin company, and we had very little cash, January 2025, I think $1.7 million, very little assets, net fixed assets were like $30 million. And in a year, we went from no cash, little assets, a Bitcoin dying business, to cash, $130 million, $140 million of net fixed assets, CapEx this year already invested of $160 million, and AI revenues that represent almost half of our total revenue. We believe that it was a transformative year and quarter as we are growing accordingly to our pace.

We started to process data bare metal rental in May very successfully, and a lot of companies came to our site to see our execution, and everybody is very impressed.

Moderator: How much revenue is recurring or contracted?

Michel Amar: Columbiana, Alabama, provides our strongest long-term contractual revenue visibility with a 10-year take-or-pay agreement. phase I represents approximately $402 million in contractual fees, increasing to approximately $1.07 billion with phase II. NeoCloudz and our other operating business also generate ongoing revenue, although contract duration and utilization vary anywhere from two to five years. We are focused on increasing the share supported by long-term commitments, five years for NeoCloudz and 10-15 years for colocation.

Moderator: Thank you. What does the December 15 Columbiana milestone represent?

Michel Amar: December 15 is a key number, and considering that we signed the deal with our tenant, Cerebras, early May, which is really few months ago, it is really incredible that we managed to design and finance and set up phase I in basically seven months. I believe we are one of a kind to be able to execute on such a short period of time. December 15, tenants will have access to the data hall, and we will start commissioning the power and all the liquid cooling systems that are basically done. It is a very important delivery milestone for us, as a landlord, after the commissioning, we can start billing.

Moderator: Thank you. What is the financial value of the Columbiana agreement over 10 years?

Michel Amar: As stated a few minutes ago, the 15 MW first phase represents about $402 million over 10 years, and the second phase of 25 MW represents an additional, a little bit over $600 million, for a total of $1.07 billion, averaging about $107 million annually.

Moderator: Thank you. Is phase II on track for March 2027 tenant access?

Michel Amar: Construction of phase II has already started. There's a lot of common denominators between phase I and phase II. The data hall has both phases in the data hall, so it's there. All the fire suppressing requirements, all the chillers are already there for both phases, phase I, phase II. All the equipment has been purchased for both phases, phase I, phase II. We designed that contract to be able to do two phases so we can financially support it no matter what. And we are very advanced in the phase II as well. We don't see any issues to be giving access to phase II in March 2027.

The commissioning will be easier because all the liquid cooling commissioning will be done, this month and early December. We believe that the type of commissioning will be shorter in March, and we expect to bill in April the full total monthly contract.

Moderator: Thank you. What additional value could the renewal options under the Cerebras contract provide?

Michel Amar: It's a little bit under $1.1 billion for the initial term, and we can go to a total of $2.5 billion as an option. We believe that the investment of a tenant is so huge, they're going to locate in that data hall billions of U.S. dollars of equipment, of servers and GPUs, and it's very costly for a tenant to move out. Not only it's costly, it's their economic interest to stay as much as they can, as the power shells are very scarce these days. The value in our opinion will be much greater in 10 years from now.

Moderator: Thank you. Where does the proposed $200 million financing stand?

Michel Amar: It's interesting because earlier, we had an approved financing that I denied in July from one of the biggest world institutions, because I didn't like the terms. Then we decided to deny an approved financing to move and syndicate it through Goldman Sachs, which saved us on this financing about 2 points, which is incredible. As the interest rate went up, we managed to save 2 points on the financing. We are pretty close to close the financing. We got the loan documents, so we expect to close in the near future, definitely in Q4 2026, but we are pushing to close earlier.

I think we are one of the best candidates for a lender, as we invested our own capital, we have cash, and we are executing on time. We're a pretty good candidate for a lender. But we managed to be in single-digit interest, which is rare these days.

Moderator: Thank you. How will the approximately $350 million 2027 capital plan be funded while minimizing dilution?

Michel Amar: Our funding strategy combines customer deposits and prepayments. With the crisis that happened in the last four, five weeks, which was torture for our stock price, but [inaudible] company, the entire market went down. It also gave us an opportunity to ask for bigger deposits on new contracts. Now the few discussions that we have, and we're finalizing to set up new contracts, we are getting much more than we used to six months ago. If six months ago, a tenant or a bare metal renter was willing to give you 15%, now they are willing to give you 30%, and sometimes up to 50%, and sometimes, as we are negotiating now, up to 70%.

Then, financing from the customer perspective becomes so easy for us to grow because we achieved something incredible. We are credible. We are not a company that talks about power and may have a connected power with a grid in 2028, 2029. We are executing, and a lot of very big companies came to our sites, very large companies came to our sites and understand that we are for real, that we are credible. Now they are willing to support us and give us bigger deposits and help us finance our growth. That's the major accomplishment that we are realizing by execution.

Moderator: Thank you. What supports the $250 million-$300 million annualized revenue target?

Michel Amar: We already have about $143 million on the contract, and the remaining $107 million-$150 million are expected from our development of the other sites through colocation or GPU capacity. If we had an open bank checkbook, we could have done it for Q1 or Q2, but considering the market condition and our financial discipline, if we want to go fast, we have to walk slowly. We have to be careful. We survived all the Bitcoin cycle years since 2016. We always survived. We do not want to be exposed to a financial issue by growing too fast. We have time.

There is plenty of market share for us to get, and we believe that Q3 will be in the run rate of what we announced.

Moderator: Thank you. Can operations generate sustained positive cash flow?

Michel Amar: Let us talk about Q3. We generated $4.7 million of positive cash flow in Q3. True, with the help of a $12 million non-refundable tenant infrastructure contribution, and we believe that next quarter, we will also be cash flow positive between the business and additional non-refundable contribution, a bigger one this time because of the relationship from 15 MW-25 MW. Then we are live, then we have cash flow coming in. We believe we are in a good position today to create positive cash flow.

Moderator: Thank you. What drove NeoCloudz growth, and are its margins sustainable?

Michel Amar: We started with bare metal GPU rental, and you saw the increase of 127% sequentially to $2.46 million as NeoCloudz scaled. The opportunity of bare metal and GPU as a service and Token Factory is to increase your revenue per megawatt and your margins per megawatt. Considering the environment, the political environment, and by the way, we are very lucky to be in Alabama.

I was at the election night last Tuesday with the governor-elect, the coach, Tommy Tuberville, which I have a great relationship, and he reiterated directly to me that he's welcoming data centers in Alabama, and he's welcoming our contribution, he's welcoming our job creation, he's welcoming the incredible science and technology of AI that will save lives, cure cancer, and cure most of incurable diseases. So he's very pro-technology and is welcoming growth in Alabama. We're very excited because we definitely can expand in Alabama and contribute to the communities and create value to these communities by creating a lot of jobs. Now, in our campus, in a very small city, we have about 250 construction workers.

Highly paid jobs that are going to restaurants, that are going to hotels, that are creating an economic boom locally. So, it's really interesting, and we're really excited to be in Alabama there. And to go back to this business of maximizing and optimizing per megawatt, considering the scarcity of power and the upcoming regulations of power, where they're going to start to basically state-to-state limit powers below 50 MW, I don't think that you're going to see 500 MW campus or 1 GW campus. I think the strategy is more into the 50 MW, 25 MW campuses, which is very big if you do GPU bare metal. Very big revenues.

Today, bare metal, we can book short-term B300 at $775 per hour, bare metal. If we do tokens, it's $15-$20 per hour equivalent, per token. So, the pricing is booming on the GPU as a service. And it makes sense because the bottleneck, the struggle, the constraint is permitted available power. And why it creates an opportunity for a small company like ours is that we do own the power. We understand all stacks from power generation to Token Factory, which is very rare. Not a lot of companies generate power and are able to sell tokens. And every megawatt now, we know how to maximize the revenue and the value.

We can get easily $2 million-$3 million a month per megawatt. So, if I just convert 50 MW, that's $100 million a month. Okay? At very, by the way, very high margins, 85%-90% margins. Our catalyst is execution, which we are 95% there, and financial discipline, and we are an incredible company.

Energous (WATT) Q3 2026 Earnings Call Transcript

📅 Nov 13, 2026  |  Category: Technology  |  Read Source →

Pilots are giving way to rollouts as a major program eyes its first full conversion in 2027.

Need a quote from a Motley Fool analyst? Email [email protected]

Energous Corporation (WATT -11.27%) reported consecutive quarterly revenue growth and gross margin improvement as the company moved from technology validation to commercial deployment. Management stated that the company is developing a physical data layer for enterprise artificial intelligence (AI) by combining wireless power with battery-free sensing. The company reported an expansion in its proof-of-concept pipeline through the AWS channel and expects to convert a major program to full deployment in the first quarter of 2027.

Operator: Good day, and welcome to the Energous Wireless Power Solutions Third Quarter 2026 Financial Results Conference Call. [Operator Instructions] Please note, this event is being recorded. As a reminder, during today's call, the company will make forward-looking statements. These statements are subject to inherent risks and uncertainties detailed in the company's filings with the Securities and Exchange Commission. Actual results may differ materially from those anticipated. Except as otherwise required by federal law, Energous disclaims any obligation to publicly release updates or revisions to any forward-looking statements to reflect changes in expectations. I would now like to turn the conference call over to Mallorie Burak Chief Executive Officer and Chief Financial Officer of Energous.

Mallorie Burak: Thank you, and welcome, everyone, to the Energous Wireless Power Solutions Third Quarter 2026 Earnings Call. For those newer to the Energous story, I would encourage you to review the replay of our first quarter call, which provides a full company overview and our second quarter call, which lays out the commercial framework I will build upon today. I will keep the background brief and focus on what has changed and what is building. At its core, Energous is an end-to-end Ambient IoT platform delivering battery-free sensing and real-time operational intelligence. We are building the physical data layer foundation for enterprise AI.

By combining wireless power, battery-free sensing and intelligent data infrastructure, our products enable continuous real-time visibility into assets, inventory and operations, helping enterprises move from simply tracking what's happening to predicting what might happen next and taking action. This intelligence enables companies to mitigate annualized losses stemming from spoilage, inventory shrinkage as well as operating costs resulting from manual tracking methods like RFID. Since pivoting our strategic vision away from consumer IoT to enterprise wireless power solutions in 2022, our focus has been to move from technology validation to commercial adoption at the largest enterprises in the world.

While our sequential quarterly revenue growth underscores the commercial adoption of our product offerings is underway, we believe that the fourth quarter will more clearly demonstrate that the rate of adoption is ramping as our largest customers are expanding in scope and use cases, our shipments continue to build, and our pipeline is beginning to produce specific conversion milestones. In terms of today's agenda, I will start with our operating highlights and the financial headlines, including our gross margin recovery path. Giampaolo will then give a detailed update on each of our customer programs and the sales pipeline, including how programs convert from proof of concept to deployment.

Greg will walk through the financials in detail, and I will return briefly with a few concluding thoughts. Since our Power Bridge transmitter began shipping in 2024, through September 30, 2026, we have shipped over 6,500 transmitters, primarily driven by demand for our 2-watt Power Bridge Pro. The sequential increases in quarterly shipments that began in the fourth quarter of 2025, reflects the shift from technology validation to commercial adoption of our wireless power networks. The third quarter continued the trend of consecutive quarterly revenue growth. We recorded $3.4 million of revenue in the third quarter up approximately 10% from the $3.1 million reported in the second quarter.

We expect sequential growth to continue and to accelerate in the fourth quarter. Gross margin for the third quarter was approximately 14%, a meaningful improvement from the second quarter as the factors we described last quarter began to ease and our July price increase took effect. On our last conference call, we described the retooling, component costs and customer prioritization factors that reduced second quarter margins and we said that we expected margins to progressively recover. Greg will cover the third quarter drivers of this, including the effect of the July price increase. Also on our second quarter conference call, we shared that we were working towards limited production volume at our overseas contract manufacturer during the quarter.

In the third quarter, however, all units sourced came from our U.S. contract manufacturer. The reason stems back to what we described last quarter. The production line upgrades needed to -- needed at our overseas contract manufacturer to support the enhanced product design were still being completed during the third quarter so volume there has not yet begun. Despite that, we still recognized improvement in our gross margin primarily attributable to the price increases implemented in July. Looking to the fourth quarter, both our U.S. and overseas contract manufacturers are producing at volumes.

As a result, we expect fourth quarter gross margins to improve quarter-over-quarter, and we estimate returning to our historical hardware margin levels in the mid-30% range during the first half of 2027. Our long-term objective remains achieving gross margins of 40% or better. I will now turn it over to Giampaolo, our Chief Strategy and Growth Officer, to discuss our customer programs, our pipeline and the commercial dynamics we are seeing. Giampaolo?

Giampaolo Marino: Thank you, Mallorie. I intend to cover for areas today and update on fewer of our active commercial deployment, where our proof-of-concept program stand and where we expect them to convert. What we are hearing from enterprise customers about data and how our software platform feeds into the equation. Our enterprise adoption is advancing across manufacturing, retail and cold chain. The most important shift this year is that the focus is moving from pilots to broader deployments. While at the same time, our customers are expanding the use cases they run on our network, including sale of inventory condition monitoring and asset tracking.

Active commercial deployments, starting with our largest retail customer portion and companies, we are now working with them on additional use cases, which they intend to begin rolling out across their superstore format in 2027. This customer has over 500 superstores in the United States and the scale per location matter. A supercenter requires over 125 bridges compared with roughly 10 to 15 bridges for a standard store. The same customers warehouse cloud division is planned to deploy across approximately 150 locations at about 15 bridges per location. We expect that rollout to begin in early 2027 and continue throughout the year.

Looking out over the next 12 months, we expect demand from these Fortune 10 customers to continue 2 levels. This customer is also evaluating a potential expansion into Canada across their regular retail format, which will require new regulatory approvals for the Power Bridge Pro in that jurisdiction. Infrastructure requirements in that region are still being determined. Fleet and distribution center application continue to be explored, both involve more complex infrastructure, which our customer is assessing. Turning to our Fortune 10 e-commerce customer. Based on that customer's current planning, we continue to expect growth over the next 12 months. Deployment had and will continue across Europe.

We expect to see acceleration in deployment in the U.S. where we are still in the very early stage of opportunity completion with this customer. Use case continued to expand as well and we are now looking at an expansion opportunities in virtual. While we are limited in what we can share in greater detail, in 2027, we expect revenue from this customer to exceed 2026. We share both of these updates today to provide confidence to our investors that we believe our revenue base looking forward is stronger and set up for continued growth. Our largest customers are strong supporters of our technology and are still in the early stages of their adoption cycle.

Because it is the question we frequently received, I would like to spend some time discussing our sales conversion and where active programs currently stand. Our framework has not changed. A program begins with an alignment phase to agree on goals and metrics, typically lasting about a month. A proof of concept follows generally lasting up to 3 months. Successful programs might then expand to multiple trials or move to commercial deployment. With the current sales cycle spanning approximately 6 to 9 months versus 18 to 24 months historically, programs now move faster, but we judge progress by stage advancement not by the number of trials. We also recognize that we have not announced named customers at this year.

Many of our customers do not permit disclosure, and we will not name a customer without permission. What we can do is tell you where some of the more advanced programs are by stage. Several end-to-end proof-of-concept trials are currently active. The first is with a large semi-perishable consumer product manufacturer. It is nearly completion, and we currently expect the customers to move to deployment in the first quarter of 2027. If that happens as planned, it will be the first of our end-to-end solution programs to convert from our proof-of-concept pipeline to a deployment, creating a recurring revenue stream generated from our end-to-end solution, anchored by the monitoring and analytics provided by our SaaS platform e-Compass.

The second proof of concept, which we introduced last quarter is with our regional supercenter and grocery chain operating hundreds of stores across the Midwestern United States. Building on that data generated in the initial phase, we are now working with the customers to define Phase II around measurable business outcomes, reducing shrink, lowering scanning, hardware and maintenance costs and improving labor efficiency and supporting e-commerce growth. The planned scope includes tracking goods between distribution centers and stores, improving shipment manifest accuracy and integrating physical world data into existing enterprise system and analytics. The focus is on validating how our end-to-end solution can deliver operational and financial value, while fitting into the customers' day-to-day workflows.

Beyond those 2 opportunities, we are exploring additional end-to-end proof-of-concept initiatives: one, notably in the health care space, where the Fortune 10 health care retailer had previously completed a proof of concept through a channel partner has chosen to reengage directly to test our full scope solution. Our strategic partnership with AWS continues to strengthen, serving as an important channel for expanding Energous' enterprise reach and accelerating adoption of our end-to-end ambient IoT platform. Customer engagement through the AWS channel has reached a new high with a growing number of active proof-of-concept programs and joint opportunities.

This momentum is also reflected in the AWS recently increasing its reporting highlight customer launches from more than 50% to more than 100, an encouraging indicator of broader customer adoption across the AWS partner ecosystem. Energous continues to expand its presence in the AWS marketplace with 3 qualified device alongside our e-Compass software platform and our e-Sense battery-free sensing technology. Together, this offering provides enterprise customers with access to our integrated wireless power, sensing and data infrastructure solution. Looking ahead, we expect our collaboration with AWS to remain an important driver of pipeline development, customer acquisition and future development opportunity as joint co-selling initiative progress and existing program advanced towards the broader commercialization.

Our national quick service restaurant program remains active. As we discussed last quarter, this customer completed its initial proof of concept in food preparation and cold storage environment. They continue to evaluate different solutions, although we are only supplying the PowerBridge transmitters for this particular proof of concept, we believe the value proposition of using battery-free wireless technology remains a compelling solution for this use case. Our data strategy. As enterprise expand AI into physical operations, they will need reliable, timely data about what is happening across their assets, inventory and facilities. Energous strategy is to address that need through an integrated solution spending persistent energy, connected sensing and trusted physical data infrastructure.

Our focus is on delivering actionable data into customers' existing systems with customer retaining control of their data and how it is used. Central to this strategy is the evolution of the e-Compass into our Layer 3 platform. The software layer that turns physical world sensor readings into contextualized usable data and events for enterprise applications. Our road map includes enterprise APIs real-time event processing, AI assisted infrastructure management and trusted physical data service. Together, these capabilities are intended to help customers assess data quality, monitor infrastructure performance and integrate physical intelligence into their operational scale.

We are pursuing strategic integration and partnership with enterprise IoT, supply chain and AI software providers to extend the value of our platform beyond hardware deployment. By delivering persistent trusted and contextualized physical world data directly into enterprise intelligence and decision-making systems, Energous aims to enable continuous operational visibility, predictive insight and increasingly automated workflow at scale. We believe that this strategy will create opportunities for recurring revenue through infrastructure management and physical data service, while allowing our partner to deliver specialized analytics, applications and business outcomes.

Although this initiative remains at an early stage, with no assurance of recurring resulting agreement or revenue, our strategic direction is clear: To establish Energous as the foundational physical data infrastructure platform for enterprise AI and physical AI. I will now turn it over to Greg.

Gregory Sadikoff: Thank you, Giampaolo. Good afternoon. I will now review Energous' financial results for the third quarter and 9 months ended September 30, 2026. Third quarter financial results. Revenue for the 3 and 9 months ended September 30, 2026, was approximately $3.4 million and $9.6 million, respectively, versus approximately $1.3 million and $2.6 million in the same period in 2025, increases of 167% and 270%, respectively over the same prior year period. Third quarter revenue increased approximately 10% sequentially over the second quarter of 2026. Gross margin improved from 3% in the second quarter of 2026 to 14% for the 3 months ended September 30, 2026.

As our offshore contract manufacturer ramps production, we believe gross margins will continue to show improvement in the fourth quarter and into 2027. The company has maintained its quality performance record with 0 product returns since commercial production of its PowerBridge Pro began in 2024. Ensuring the highest level of product quality remains a key priority for the company as we work towards widespread adoption of our technology. The net loss for the 9 months ended September 30, 2026, was $7.1 million compared to a loss of $8.3 million for the same prior year period, a 14% improvement year-over-year.

The narrowing loss year-to-date reflects our commitment to remain focused on exercising fiscal discipline as we work towards achieving profitability and cash flow breakeven. To underscore this commitment, since the current management team took over in early 2024, approximately $9 million of annualized expenses has been cut contributing to a 50% improvement in net loss for the 9 months ended September 30, 2026 compared to the same period in 2024. As we look ahead, our primary objective is to strike the right balance between driving sustained long-term growth and accelerating our path to profitability.

We believe that growth and profitability are not mutually exclusive and maintaining this balanced strategic framework serves to build a scalable business model is well positioned to deliver value to our shareholders. As of September 30, 2026, the company's cash and cash equivalents were $28.8 million. No shares were sold under the ATM program during the third quarter. I will now turn the call back to Mallorie.

Mallorie Burak: Thank you, Greg. Regarding Greg's reference to the ATM, I would like to mention that while our ATM facility remains in place, we have made no use of it since the first quarter. During the last 2 quarters' calls, I committed that there were no plans for additional ATM usage this year, and that commitment has not changed. We believe our cash position at the end of the quarter of $28.8 million is sufficient to support our commercial programs and we remain confident in our ability to execute without additional equity financing. Before we move to Q&A, I'd like to reiterate what I hope investors take away from today's call.

First, we expect sequential revenue growth to accelerate in the fourth quarter. Both contract manufacturers are expected to contribute to our unit volume, and we anticipate gross margins will continue to improve. Second, we anticipate continued growth during the next 12 months. We believe 2027 demand from our largest retail customer to at least approximate 2026 levels with the warehouse club rollout beginning early in the year, and we believe 2027 revenue from our e-commerce customer will exceed that reported in 2026. Third, we feel strongly about our competitive position and the direction of the business. We hold more than 300 issued patents in regulatory approvals in over 110 countries.

We believe the macro backdrop is a tailwind for our business as enterprises look for persistent energy and trusted data to support physical AI. Our pipeline continues to grow. Active proof-of-concept engagements through our AWS channel are the new high. Our partners are doing more, and we expect to show the first conversion from our end-to-end solution proof of concept pipeline to deployment in the first quarter. Revenue, shipments, margins and pipeline are all moving in the right direction. The market is beginning to recognize that physical AI requires the ability to continuously generate trusted data from the physical world, and we believe that capability begins with persistent energy. We believe it begins with Energous technology.

As physical AI evolves, one reality is becoming clear. Intelligence is only as reliable as the physical data that powers it. Continuous data requires persistent sensing and persistent sensing requires persistent energy. This is where we believe Energous becomes foundational to the physical AI ecosystem. We are grateful for your continued support, and we will now open the call for questions.

Operator: [Operator Instructions] Our first question comes from Jon Hickman of Ladenburg Thalmann.

Jon Hickman: So can you -- maybe this is a question for Giampaolo, but can you give us some kind of year-over-year pacing of new customers? Yes, either like proof of concept?

Jon Hickman: Yes. Like what's the year-over-year growth rate here in the number of customers or proof of concepts or I don't know, how you want it to?

Mallorie Burak: Yes. I think -- so if you recall back to the second quarter of 2026, when 5 customers made up 74%, I recall, yes. So we expect that to grow. I think as...

Mallorie Burak: Yes. So we expect the customer base to grow. We're expanding our proof of concept pipeline but also the proof of concepts that are active right now. Terac: Product Marketing Managers: GTM Brief Review for Bike Repair Tool

We're running a paid study on go-to-market strategies tailored to independent retail operations. Specifically, we are evaluating a new appointment management tool designed for independent bicycle repair shops. This research helps us refine our initial product positioning and identify hidden risks before launch.

You will complete an asynchronous, remote review of a short go-to-market brief. During this task, you will read through our target audience assumptions, proposed messaging, and launch plan. Next, you will write a short report that clearly separates unsubstantiated assumptions from hard evidence. Finally, you will recommend one low-cost validation step to test our riskiest assumption without conducting new market outreach.

We welcome Product Marketing Managers, GTM Strategists, and early-stage Product Managers with experience in retail software or small business tools. Candidates should have a strong grasp of market validation techniques and startup strategy. Familiarity with independent bike shops or local service businesses is a strong plus, though not strictly required.

Read and evaluate a short go-to-market strategy brief for a bike shop appointment tool

Identify and separate unproven assumptions from backed evidence within the document

Write a concise written report summarizing your strategic findings

Propose one cheap, actionable validation step to test the core strategy

Professional experience in product marketing, go-to-market strategy, or product management

Strong analytical skills for evaluating business plans and marketing briefs

Ability to communicate strategic feedback clearly in a written format

Familiarity with retail operations or local service business software is a plus

Terac is building the world's largest pool of vetted human experts for AI. Researchers, AI labs, and product teams use Terac to recruit, screen, and pay study participants across industries, languages, and skill sets.

Learn more at terac.com or on YouTube at @jointerac.

CapsLock is a global IT marketing company that pioneers unique, scalable customer acquisition solutions for B2C clients. We solve complex sales challenges across a wide range of industries, primarily for large North American partners. By integrating our expertise in Digital Marketing, IT, Design, AI-driven analytics, proprietary MarTech, and sales consulting, we deliver powerful, data-informed customer acquisition solutions.

At CapsLock, people, technology, and forward-thinking innovation lie at the heart of everything we do. Our diverse, global team, fluent in over 10 languages, thrives on open collaboration and the exchange of new ideas, pushing boundaries to ensure our clients' success.

Our creative team produces hundreds of video ads, images and ad copy variations every month, and we test them across our campaigns to find out what works best. Behind all of that is a fast-moving pipeline, and we’re looking for a Creative Marketing Project Coordinator to keep it running smoothly.

You’ll be working with a team of 15–25 creative strategists, video editors, designers and media buyers. You’ll turn ideas and requests into clear tasks, keep everyone aligned on deadlines, review the final work before it goes live, and make sure every asset gets launched and tested. Many of our workflows are already automated, and we’d love someone who enjoys finding smarter ways to work as we grow.

If you love bringing order to a busy environment, have a sharp eye for detail and are curious about the creative side of marketing, this role is a great place to grow. You’ll also be invited to brainstorming sessions and can contribute your own ideas to the creative process.

Tare: “Senza il Covid penso avremmo vinto quello Scudetto. Lotito e Inzaghi…”

📅 Nov 13, 2026  |  Category: Technology  |  Read Source →

Ai microfoni del podcast Sky Calcio Unplugged condotto da Gianluca Di Marzio è stato intervistato l’ex diesse della Lazio, Igli Tare

Ai microfoni del podcast Sky Calcio Unplugged condotto da Gianluca Di Marzio è stato intervistato l’ex diesse della Lazio, Igli Tare.

CLICCA QUI E SEGUI LAZIOPRESS.IT ANCHE SU INSTAGRAM

Sono felice per la Lazio di oggi. La società ha creato una squadra competitiva e ha scelto Gattuso, che ha creato un clima positivo: tutti gli vogliono bene. Questa è una società con grande storia, rimarrò sempre legato alla Lazio. Ho un legame forte con questo club.

Abbiamo avuto degli approcci ma era più un'idea di Lotito. Conosco Rino, ho un rapporto molto bello con lui. Sono molto contento per lui.

Penso di sì. Era una storia molto bella. Prima della pandemia eravamo devastanti, dopo 20' eravamo già 2-0 o 3-0 per noi". Una chiacchiera significativa con Inzaghi, al tempo allenatore della Lazio: "All'inizio della stagione parlai con Simone e mi disse: ‘Igli, sei preoccupato?’. E io: ‘Io per niente, sono straconvinto che lotteremo per vincere lo Scudetto’. E lui: ‘Ma tu sei matto’. E io: ‘Fidati che sarà un'annata particolare ma molto bella per noi.

Penso che Ciro sia un ragazzo unico. Molto sensibile, aveva bisogno dell'ambiente che gli dava fiducia. E poi è stato straordinario, ha scritto la storia della Lazio. Un giocatore irripetibile.

Parliamo di un centroavanti fenomenale per il modo di interpretare il gioco e nella Lazio, per vari motivi, non ha dato quello che poteva dare. Aveva un aereo privato con destinazione Parigi al PSG. Ma lui ha rifiutato per venire alla Lazio. Ha rifiutato anche più del doppio di stipendio che gli è stato proposto da altre parti per venire in Italia.

Lui aveva l'ambizione di giocare il Mondiale 2014 e all’epoca nessun club gli dava un contratto con più di un anno. C'erano Galatasaray, Valencia, ma sempre per un anno. Io gli ho dato un contratto di tre anni, ho convinto anche Lotito. Quando abbiamo firmato tutti mi hanno massacrato perché stavamo pagando uno di 32 anni che aveva vissuto un anno negativo con qualche infortunio. Ma dopo il primo/secondo allenamento tutti hanno capito che era un fuoriclasse.

Con lui ho un legame molto speciale, abbiamo giocato insieme. Ha intrapreso una carriera che per tanti era una sorpresa ma per me mai. La reputo una persona molto curiosa, intelligente nella gestione. Poi è cresciuto tanto con l’esperienza alla Lazio e all'Inter. Quando tornerà, non so se in Italia o all’estero, penso che sarà un allenatore completo. L'esperienza più bella che ho vissuto? Con Delio Rossi. Ero giocatore con lui per tre annie quando Lotito gli comunicò che io sarei stato il nuovo DS lui rimase sorpreso e disse 'allora mi vuoi mandare via?’. Cambiare da giocatore a diventare "il capo di un allenatore" era una sorpresa anche per me, non mi aspettavo questa scelta di Lotito.

© 2015 LazioPress.it | Tutti i diritti sono riservati | Testata giornalistica con autorizzazione del Tribunale Civile di Roma numero 13/2015.

È vietata la copia, totale o parziale, degli articoli senza la relativa citazione. I responsabili saranno soggetti a provvedimenti secondo la legge.

Scarica l’app ufficiale e ricevi tutte le news della Lazio in tempo reale.
