Ecopetrol (EC) Q3 2026 Earnings Call Transcript

📅 Nov 13, 2026  |  Category: Technology  |  Read Source →

Record refining margins met external headwinds this quarter.

Need a quote from a Motley Fool analyst? Email [email protected]

Ecopetrol S.A. (EC +0.72%) reported a significant increase in net income for the third quarter of 2026, supported by the integration of its newly acquired Brazilian subsidiary, Brava Energia, and record high refining margins. Management identified five strategic priorities for the company, including reserve additions, strengthening gas supply, and operational efficiency across the business group. The company completed a $2 billion bond issuance to optimize its debt profile and refinance short-term obligations related to its international expansion. Leadership emphasized a strategy focusing on energy security and disciplined capital allocation to restore market confidence and drive sustainable value creation.

Operator: Good morning. My name is Natalia, and I will be your operator today. Welcome to Ecopetrol's earnings conference call, in which we will discuss the main financial and operating results of the third quarter of 2026. There will be a questions and answers session at the end of the presentation. Before we begin, it is important to mention that the comments in this call by Ecopetrol's senior management include projections of the company's future performance. These projections do not constitute any commitment as to future results, nor do they take into account risks or uncertainties that could materialize.

As a result, Ecopetrol assumes no responsibility in the event that future results are different from the projections shared on this conference call. The call will be led by Joaquín Gutiérrez, CEO of Ecopetrol; Camilo Barco, CFO; Elsa Jaimes, Acting Executive Vice President of Hydrocarbons; and Armando Cuello, Executive Vice President of Energies for the Transition. Thank you for your attention. Mr. Gutiérrez, you may begin your conference.

Joaquín Gutiérrez: Good morning, everyone, and thank you for joining us. Welcome to the first earnings call under the current administration of Ecopetrol and its leadership team. I would like to thank the board of directors for their trust and confidence in appointing me to lead the company. We will work in close coordination with our majority shareholder and government authorities within the framework of our corporate governance standards and the best interests of all shareholders. I'd like to extend a special greeting to our investors, shareholders, and other stakeholders, and reaffirm my commitment to this great project that we have called the new era of Ecopetrol.

This is not a slogan, but rather a strategic direction and a commitment to Colombia, a new era that is already underway to foster the development of the industry, strengthen Ecopetrol's leadership as a driving force of Colombia's energy sector, and unlock strategic projects that are critical to energy security, competitiveness, and growth for both the company and the country. I take on this responsibility, convinced that Ecopetrol possesses high-quality assets, an integrated value chain, recognized technical capabilities, and an exceptional workforce. Through our initial reviews across the organization, we have identified concrete opportunities to reorganize operations more efficiently across the business group, increase reserve additions, develop new gas alternatives, and expand our ability to generate value.

Our path forward can be summarized through five priorities. First, increase reserves by prioritizing profitable barrels, advancing exploration in licensed areas, and evaluating new growth opportunities both inside and outside Colombia. Second, actively participate in the short-term solutions that Colombia requires to strengthen gas and energy supply and contribute to the country's energy security through a new competitive gas offering for regasification alternatives. Third, advance the development of offshore resources to convert their potential into production and value. While fully respecting the fundamental right to prior consultation, we are working with government authorities to better coordinate prior consultation and environmental permitting processes.

Fourth, strengthen legal certainty, restoring market credibility and confidence in Ecopetrol through consistent execution while attracting foreign investment to support our growth projects. Fifth, strengthen our operations through a focus on operational efficiency, prioritizing profitability, cash generation, and competitive returns. We are already reinforcing cost discipline measures. At the parent company level, we will eliminate duplicate activities, reduce rework, and optimize administrative workloads to capture efficiencies. In addition, since my arrival, we have been working alongside the Ministry of Finance on mechanisms to facilitate the monetization of Fuel Price Stabilization Fund and tax receivables through payment offsets.

I would like to highlight the inclusion by the national government of the FEPC-related appropriations in the 2027 national budget, as well as the recent payment corresponding to the third and fourth quarters of the 2025 balance. These actions provide greater visibility regarding funding resources for next year and contribute positively to the group's financial management and liquidity. Likewise, it's important to highlight the removal of the attachment risk associated with the dispute on fuel imports with DIAN, following the authorities' clarification that enforced collections cannot proceed while the matter remains subject to judicial review. The merits of the dispute continue to be addressed through the courts.

Our commitment is to build on the group's strengths and accelerate sustainable value creation. Let's move to the next slide. This agenda requires leadership, governance, and an organization aligned around common objectives. Over the past two months, the general shareholders meeting elected a renewed board of directors, which subsequently established its committees, all chaired by independent members. In addition, new leaders have joined strategic areas of the company and will support the execution of the five priorities I just outlined. We are advancing strategic initiatives for the industry through coordination with government authorities, regional stakeholders, and other interest groups to accelerate projects that are critical to both the company's future and the country's energy security.

Market confidence is not built through announcements. It's built through consistent execution, legal certainty, and measurable results. This is how we intend to lead the rebirth and future of Ecopetrol. Today, we are reporting solid third quarter results that demonstrate the company's ability to capitalize on improved market conditions. Let's move to the next slide. The key message defining this quarter's performance is that our integrated business model once again demonstrated its ability to capture value. As president, I inherit challenges like mature fields and the lower base production in certain assets. However, supported by the strength of our refining, transportation, and commercial businesses, we have delivered strong financial results that provide a solid foundation for achieving our objectives.

During the quarter, we reached a consolidated production of 747,000 bbl of oil equivalent per day, transported 1,000,087 bpd, and recorded refinery throughput of 412,000 bpd. Refining remained the primary value driver during the quarter, supported by a record gross refining margin of $45.6 per bbl, the highest levels ever recorded by the group. Notably, these results were achieved while significant maintenance activities were underway at the Barrancabermeja refinery, demonstrating resilience, reliability, and operational flexibility of our integrated system.

Against this backdrop, and with Brava's initial contribution following its consolidation in September, we delivered COP 4.8 trillion in net income, up 86% year-on-year, an EBITDA margin of 45%, representing an increase of 4 percentage points, and a strong cash position of COP 16.3 trillion. These results benefited from favorable market conditions and progress in our efficiency program, which accumulated COP 4.4 trillion in benefits, approximately 64% of which directly impacted EBITDA, strengthening profitability and financial flexibility across the group. We also reduced deferred production associated with external events by 91% compared to the previous quarter, reflecting a meaningful improvement in the operational security of our assets. In addition, we advanced projects that expand our future growth options.

At Sirius, we made significant progress in mitigating regulatory and socio-environmental risks that had been affecting the project's development schedule. We secured the timely approval of the amendment of the Ballena environmental permit, a key milestone to adapt gas processing infrastructure. We also continue supporting initiatives aimed at streamlining prior consultation processes and improving coordination with environmental permitting requirements, helping ensure the execution of this strategic project for Colombia's energy security. At KGG, we achieved a significant milestone with the approval of the exemption from the environmental alternatives assessment requirement for the project's flow line.

This achievement allows us to continue advancing environmental management activities and supports the development of a strategic initiative to strengthen the gas supply of the country. At Komodo, as a result of the coordinated efforts between Ecopetrol, operating partner Anadarko, and the Colombian government, ANLA enabled the drilling of the exploratory well by removing inappropriate obligations from the environmental license granted in 2024. This milestone makes the execution of a key project for the Ecopetrol Group viable. Finally, the approval of the Orca development plan in Brazil represents an important step toward unlocking an asset with the potential to deliver more than 33,000 net bpd to Ecopetrol.

All of these achievements strengthen our long-term vision and support future reserve additions and production growth. Let's move to the next slide. One of the most important milestones of the quarter was the closing of the acquisition, an issue, the start of the integration process, and the consolidation of Brava. As a result, Brazil becomes a strategic pillar of Ecopetrol's international growth. Providing new opportunities for production, reserves, and exploration. Ecopetrol's consolidated financial results for the quarter include one month of Brava's financial consolidation at 100%, contributing approximately 3% of revenue, 4% of EBITDA, and 2% of net income growth. As such, our investment in Brava generates value from the very beginning of the integration process.

Ecopetrol's third quarter 2026 production, including the full consolidation of Brava, reached 747,000 bbl of oil equivalent per day. Excluding the 49% of non-controlling interest, was approximately 728,000 bbl of oil equivalent per day, keeping us close to our full-year target. The integration phase we are now beginning is just as important as the consolidation phase we have successfully completed. Our focus will be on establishing corporate governance guidelines, integrating financial planning and reporting processes, harmonizing reserves under SEC standards, strengthening controls, and capturing operational and exploration synergies. Most importantly, we want the market to understand that Brava is not just an acquisition.

It is an investment designed to grow profitably and with discipline in Brazil, creating value for all shareholders. The recent approval of the Orca development plan reinforces this vision and strengthens our presence in one of the most important energy provinces in the Americas. Now let us give the floor to Camilo Barco, Vice President of Finance and Sustainable Value, who will provide additional details.

Camilo Barco: Thank you, President, and welcome to the Ecopetrol Group. As we have seen, Ecopetrol's integrated model has once again proven its ability to capture value. Let me walk with you now on how that performance translated into stronger financial results. During the third quarter of 2026, group revenues reached COP 35.1 trillion, up COP 5.2 trillion year-over-year. EBITDA came at COP 15.9 trillion, an increase of COP 3.5 trillion versus the prior year, while net income reached COP 4.8 trillion, up COP 2.2 trillion. Our EBITDA margin stood at 45%, a 4 percentage point improvement versus the same period of 2025. These results already reflect 100% of Brava's contribution for September, as previously discussed.

Its integration marks the beginning of a new phase in which our focus will be on disciplined value capture. The pricing environment was the main driver of these results. Over the first nine months of the year, the average Brent price rose 27%. Our crude basket increased 23%, and our product basket was up 45%. This last increase particularly benefited our refining business, whose share of the group EBITDA rose 15 percentage points year-over-year. Beyond market factors, I would highlight the integrated management of our refineries, crude slate optimization, and commercial coordination, which enabled us to capture favorable differentials and capitalize on this environment.

As a result, the group's integration delivered not only scale, but also agility and greater diversification of results. On a year-to-date basis, net income reached COP 13.7 trillion, up 83%, or roughly COP 6.2 trillion versus the same period of 2025. This includes COP 0.1 trillion contribution to net income following the Brava acquisition. Four factors drove this performance. First, pricing had a positive impact of COP 13.5 trillion. Taken together, higher Brent prices and stronger product differentials offset the weaker negotiated crude differentials. Second, external factors, including a lower exchange rate and inflationary cost pressures, had a negative impact of COP 4.1 trillion.

Third, operational factors reduced net income by COP 1.2 trillion, mainly due to lower sales volumes resulting from lower production. Fourth, a higher tax burden had an impact of COP 2.1 trillion, driven by the wealth tax and the increase in the income tax surcharge, which rose from zero in 2025 to 15% in 2026, in line with the projected Brent price. Year-to-date EBITDA totaled COP 47 trillion. Exploration and production accounted for 50%, refining for 19%, transportation for 16%, and transmission and toll roads for 15%. This mix highlights the complementary nature of our businesses and their ability to offset pressures across segments.

Turning to working capital, the FEPC received stood at COP 11.4 trillion at the end of September. This balance includes COP 1.6 trillion still standing from 2025, as well as amounts accrued between January and September 2026. Acceleration collections and identifying alternatives to limit further accumulation remains priorities to protect our liquidity. On leverage, our gross debt-to-EBITDA ratio stood at 2.1x at the group level and 1.5x excluding ISA. These levels reflect the consolidation and financing of Brava and remain within our ceiling of 2.5x. Let's move to the next slide. We closed September with a solid liquidity position and a cash balance of COP 16.3 trillion, including COP 3.1 trillion from Brava.

This position was mainly supported by operating cash generation and the collection of FEPC receivables for the third and fourth quarters of 2025. Operating cash flow reached COP 22 trillion, funding our investment plan, debt service, and dividend payments while preserving our financial flexibility. Year-to-date organic investment totaled $4.8 billion. We allocated 65% to hydrocarbons, 27% to transmission and toll roads, and 8% to energy transition businesses. Our priority was clear: ensure operational continuity, strengthen our core businesses, and advance selectively on growth projects. Of the total, 65% was deployed in Colombia and 35% in our international operations.

Within hydrocarbons, investments was mainly focused on exploration and production, particularly in assets in Meta and the Permian Basin, as well as projects in Brazil. We also invested in gas refining and the continuity of our transportation systems. In addition, inorganic investments totaled $1.4 billion, including the acquisition of Brava in Brazil, the stake in Madeira assets at ISA Brasil, and the entry contribution to the Génova project. Efficiency also contributed to value creation, totaling COP 4.4 trillion through September. Of this amount, COP 2.8 trillion benefited EBITDA, mainly in hydrocarbons, COP 0.9 trillion came from CapEx optimization, and COP 0.7 trillion from working capital. This program strengthens our margins and frees up resources for operations and growth.

On the debt side, we continue to proactively manage our liability profile to extend maturities, reduce refinancing risk, and preserve the company's liquidity and financial flexibility. In line with this approach, we completed a $2 billion international bond offering 2034. The proceeds will be used to fully refinance the short-term loan used to acquire a stake in Brava and to prepay $810 million of our 2029 bonds. This transaction replaces existing debt and does not increase our overall indebtedness. The order book reached 3.5 times the amount placed, the highest level in the last five years.

The strength and diversification of demand allowed us to achieve the tightest spread to both the sovereign and US Treasuries for an Ecopetrol international issuance for that period, with a new issue concession close to zero. These terms reflect investor confidence and our ability to access international capital markets on competitive terms. Now let's move to the next slide to show how this transaction improves our maturity profile. First, we are eliminating from the group the 2027 maturity of approximately $1.2 billion on the bridge loan taken out to acquire Brava, which brings that year's commitments down to $609 million.

Second, we expect to reduce the 2029 maturities to $1.1 billion, and we are replacing debt carrying an 8.625% coupon with a debt carrying a 7.875% coupon. Third, we are shifting these commitments to 2034, a year with no maturities, which extends the average life of our debt and better distributes our obligations over time. As a result, between 2027 and 2029, our annual maturities remain below $1.1 billion. This gives us a more balanced debt profile, lower short and medium-term refinancing needs, and the financial capacity to address the company's priorities. It is important to note that the 2029 bonds will be redeemed on November 19, 2026.

With that, I will hand it over to Elsa Jaimes, our Acting Executive Vice President of Hydrocarbons, who will walk you through our operating results.

Elsa Jaimes: Thank you, Camilo. in exploration, we continued to strengthen our resource addition and long-term growth strategy. As of September, we had drilled nine wells, achieving our annual program ahead of schedule and delivering three discoveries while advancing additional exploration activities planned for the fourth quarter. With the completion of the drilling activities at the Sandía well and Petrobras, we concluded the largest deepwater exploration campaign ever undertaken in the Colombian Caribbean. Over more than two years of continuous operations, five exploration wells were drilled, representing close to $1 billion in investment while maintaining the highest operational safety standards.

Regarding the Sirius project, we continue to advance engineering, environmental, and social studies, as well as the prior consultation process with communities within the area of influence. We remain on track to submit the environmental impact assessment for both the field development and the subsea flow line during the first quarter of 2027. Meanwhile, Orca Brazil reached a significant milestone on August 7 with the approval of its development plan of the ANP. This achievement, together with the progress made in the construction of the floating production storage and offloading vessel, or FPSO, continues to support the development of an asset expected to contribute more than 33,000 bpd of net production of Ecopetrol beginning 2029.

On the production front, we highlight the contribution of our new subsidiary, Brava Energia, which added 39,000 bpd, supporting total group production of 747,000 bpd. In Colombia, 91% reduction in deferred volumes associated with external events compared to the previous quarter supported the gradual recovery of crude oil production, partially upsetting natural field decline and other operational constraints. In Permian, performance remains aligned with its development plan, and production is expected to close within the range of 83,000 bbl of oil equivalent per day.

During the fourth quarter, we continue executing our production assurance plan, supported by new water handling capacity, the development of new wells in strategic assets across Colombia and the Permian Basin, and the advancement of water injection recovery initiatives of CPO-09. These efforts strengthen base production sustainability through targeted well intervention programs. As a result, the segment delivered solid operational and financial performance, reaching a year-to-date EBITDA margin of 41% as of September. Additionally, the incorporation of Brava's production volumes enhances the resilience of our production platform and supports the delivery of our production guidance while mitigating the impact of external events and operational restrictions experienced throughout the year.

At the same time, it expands our long-term growth runway and strengthens our capacity to create sustainable value for shareholders. In the refining segment, we delivered another exceptional quarter, reaching new all-time highs in integrated gross refining margin, EBITDA, and EBITDA margin. Favorable fuel market dynamics and our strong value capture capabilities enabled us to achieve a record gross refining margin of $45.6 per bbl, up 53% versus the previous quarter, and more than three times the level recorded a year ago. These results were achieved despite major maintenance activities carried out at the Barrancabermeja refinery, which temporarily reduced integrated throughput to 412,000 bpd.

As a result, the segment delivered a record EBITDA of COP 4.1 trillion and a historic EBITDA margin of 19%, highlighting the strength and resilience of our integrated hydrocarbons value chain. Turning to the transportation segment, we transported more than 1.1 million bpd during the quarter, maintaining system reliability and flexibility despite the major maintenance activities in refining. This performance was supported by incremental volumes, the transportation of imported crude, and the optimization of logistics corridors and inventory management. Although EBITDA decreased compared to the same period last year, primarily due to foreign exchange effects, the segment maintained solid operating performance and continued to support the flexibility and reliability of our integrated system. Let's move on to profitability and cost.

During the first nine months of 2026, the hydrocarbon segment further strengthened its profitability, primarily supported by a more favorable commodity price environment. EBITDA reached COP 39.3 trillion, a 32% increase compared to the same period of 2025, while the EBITDA margin expanded from 38%-45%. On the cost front, we continued to execute productivity, operational efficiency, and resource optimization initiatives that helped partially offset external cost pressures, particularly those associated with the appreciation of the Colombian peso, inflationary effects, and higher electricity tariffs driven by El Niño weather phenomenon. Lifting costs decreased 3.3% in local currency terms compared with the same period of the year before, reaching COP 47,192 per bbl.

Excluding foreign exchange effects, lifting costs stood at $11.4 per bbl, below the $11.8 per bbl reported a year ago, and our $12 per bbl reference target for 2026. This performance was supported by higher monetization volumes, as well as efficiencies captured across maintenance activities, operating services, digital infrastructure, and energy consumption. Similarly, refining cash costs declined by 2% in local currency terms, improving from COP 23,219 pesos to COP 22,750 per barrel. Excluding foreign exchange effects, refining cash costs stood at $5.5 per bbl compared to $5.6 per bbl n the same period of the prior year, supported by higher refinery throughput and disciplined cost management.

Cost per barrel transported increased by 9.3%, mainly reflecting higher operational requirements associated with emergency response efforts and challenging operating conditions. Overall, these results demonstrate our ability to translate a favorable market environment into stronger profitability while maintaining strict cost discipline to offset external pressures. I will now turn the call over to Armando, who will provide an update on the energies for the transition business line.

Armando Cuello: Thank you, Elsa. During 2026, we saw a significant shift in global LNG market dynamics at the beginning of the year. The market was characterized by growing supply and relatively balanced conditions, with international benchmarks like TTF and JKM at around $10-$11 per million BTUs. However, geopolitical tensions in the Middle East rapidly changed this environment, affecting flows equivalent to about 18% of global LNG supply and resulting in a market increasingly constrained by the availability of LNG molecules. This environment intensified competition between Europe and Asia for available cargoes and pushed TTF prices to $33 per million BTUs by the end of 2026.

This represented an increase of about 200%, reaching the highest level seen in the last four years and reflecting growing market concerns over security of supply. Against this backdrop, suppliers prioritized TTF index structures or sought to capture the price expectations embedded in that market, significantly reducing the availability of competitive offers for buyers such as Ecopetrol. As a result, the economic conditions observed during the period did not allow us to move forward with LNG purchases that met Ecopetrol's criteria for competitiveness, profitability, and appropriate risk management. For this reason, we maintained a disciplined approach and decided not to proceed with LNG procurement processes for the Puerto Bahía and Buenaventura terminals under current market conditions.

Nevertheless, at Ecopetrol, we continue making our best efforts to identify competitive supply opportunities while maintaining the commitments established under our sales contracts with domestic market participants and seeking to align supply availability with the scheduled startup dates of the regasification facilities. Consistent with this strategy, regasification projects continue to progress in line with their expected startup schedules. The Pacific regasification facility is approximately 90% complete and remains on track to begin operations by year-end 2026, while the Caribbean regasification facility has reached 53% completion and is expected to begin operations during the first quarter of 2027.

These initiatives, together with the development of offshore and onshore resources, will expand supply options and contribute to strengthening energy security and the reliability of Colombia's gas market. Next slide. Regarding domestic supply during the third quarter of 2026, Ecopetrol maintained its position as the only domestic producer to offer and allocate firm gas volumes to the market. During the quarter, we allocated additional volumes from Cupiagua, complemented by new production sources in Huila. These actions allowed us to make additional gas volumes available to the market, contributing to the reliability of domestic supply. Ecopetrol also actively contributed and responded promptly to operational events affecting the system.

During the scheduled maintenance of the SPEC regasification terminal between July 30 and August 3, we marketed 97 GBTUD to help meet thermal generation and essential demand. Subsequently, during the operational contingency at SPEC in September, we increased the availability of gas from Cusiana and Cupiagua by 5 GBTUD for six days, helping mitigate the impact on regulated demand and the national transportation system. More recently, on October 27, we increased supply by an additional 5 GBTUD to support the system during the contingency resulting from the scheduled curtailment associated with another SPEC shutdown. This contributed to maintaining the operational stability of the pipeline system until SPEC resumed normal operations. Next.

Regarding energy management, we continued implementing initiatives aimed at strengthening the group's competitiveness and reducing our exposure to price volatility associated with El Niño and the spot electricity market. First, through our energy efficiency program, we achieved cumulative optimization of 2.66 PJs, equivalent to almost twice the annual energy consumption of the Rubiales field, generating savings of approximately COP 68 billion. This allowed us to reduce the group's energy requirements and consequently our exposure to increased energy supply costs. In addition, the group's renewable energy portfolio reached 1,210 MW, including assets in operation and under construction and development, as well as market-based power supply contracts.

Our operating assets continue to generate efficiencies, delivering savings of COP 6.1 billion during the period, driven by the lower cost of energy consumed compared with market purchasing alternatives. During the quarter, we continued to make progress on the Windpeshi wind project, which reached 25% overall completion, development of the interconnection line, and securing the land required for project execution. In addition, together with our partner, AES, we continue structuring the JK1 and JK2 wind projects. Our financial advisors, IDB and Bancolombia, are currently conducting due diligence, and we expect to reach financial close and FID during the first quarter of 2027.

These initiatives, together with our self-generation capacity and contracting in the wholesale electricity market, allowed us to cover 92.6% of the group's energy demand, reducing our exposure to the spot market and strengthening operational resilience under climate-related stress scenarios. Through a combination of conventional self-generation on-site and remote renewable self-generation and active contracting management, our purchases in the wholesale electricity market were made at average prices of 8.5% below regulated market benchmarks, contributing to greater stability in the group's energy costs. Overall, these results reflect Ecopetrol's commitment to energy security, the timely development of strategic infrastructure, the incorporation of new energy sources, and the delivery of operational efficiencies that strengthen the sustainability and competitiveness of our business.

Camilo Barco: Steffania, good morning, and thank you for your questions. This is Camilo Barco. Basically, I would think that this question is more for our majority shareholders to answer. It is a decision that should be made by the national government to evaluate what to do with its property over Ecopetrol. We really have no comments on this. Nothing different to what you have mentioned. It would have surely a positive effect on the capital's market related to the floating of the company. But again, when it comes to the decision made by the government and its share or its stake in Ecopetrol, we cannot answer here what the government will do.

Operator: Let us continue with Ricardo Sandoval from Cibest. Mr. Sandoval, the floor is yours.

Ricardo Sandoval: Hello, everyone. Thank you for the call, and the best wishes for Joaquín. I have a question. The conversion of the oil pipe that was set to be made to give feasibility to the new regasification of the Caribbean. I mean, is that conversion something that will take away capability to take out future volumes in the Magdalena Medio and in the western mountain range? Is that conversion still underway as we have been talking about the Puerto Bahía? Is there a way to get out of this? It goes really against what the government wants to promote fracking at the most. That is my question. Thank you.

Nubia Stella: Good morning. This is Nubia Stella. I am the Acting President of Cenit Ricardo, thank you for your question. We, as leaders of the mixing group, are exploring different alternatives to enable the logistics necessary to develop the fields. Today, we have not ruled out any option. We are exploring and reviewing all the options that we have. As of today, we are still working on this without any specific decision on a particular project. Thank you.

Armando Cuello: I am Armando Cuello, VP for Energies of Transition. Regarding the Puerto Bahía regasification system, we are doing well. We are advancing. We think that the first two months of next year will be commissioned, and we think it is fundamental for the country's energy security. We will not have the LNG. We will not be depending only on the SPEC regasification. We are not going to be pulling out. We think it is very important for the country and for Ecopetrol. Thank you.

Mauricio Gutiérrez: Ricardo, good morning. This is Mauricio Gutiérrez, VP of Strategy and New Businesses. Regarding your consideration on Puerto Bahía and the non-conventional fields, our observations are focused on the idea that both projects contribute to the energy safety of the country, and not necessarily advance against each other or not regarding each other. With this, the opportunities of non-conventional fields in Magdalena Medio open great opportunities, which reflect the commitment of this administration to non-conventional fields with two new reserve sources, and the regasification system of Puerto Bahía will guarantee the supply of gas that we have needed, where we will be needing in that short and medium term prior to the commissioning of Sirius, which is strategic to us.

We cannot consider that they go against each other, and both projects are complementary. They do not substitute each other, and allows us to guarantee the energy safety in the country. Thank you for your question.

Operator: Last question comes from Alejandra Andrade from JPMorgan. Alejandra,

Alejandra Andrade: thank you for taking my question. Specifically, I would like to ask, you already made an issuance of bonds and to refinance the Brava loan and to pay bonds that matured in 2029, but are you considering to do something more in 2027 in the international capital market, or are you waiting for an option of that sort?

Camilo Barco: Alejandra, good morning, and thank you for your question. Let me take this opportunity to thank especially the analysts and the representatives of the bondholders and the investors' communities that have shown us in this issuance their trust and optimism over Ecopetrol. I said we are very happy.It was an issue that was interesting, not only with the amount offered, but with the demand that was so significant that exceeded 3.5 times the capital offered and led to conditions that you know well, that are very favorable for Ecopetrol with historical records over the US Treasuries and within the curve of the sovereign bonds with a minimum difference of 80 basis points over the debt of Colombia.

We are happy, satisfied. It was a significant transaction. With this, we believe that in 2026, we have defined the capital structure. Towards the future, it is important to mention what we have said before. We are monitoring constantly the market. We are evaluating all of the alternatives.Surely, interesting opportunities for financing will rise, not only for organic investments, but also for the inorganic opportunities that our CEO referred to, and our purpose to keep growing will give way to new opportunities that will be evaluated, especially if looking for an optimal financing structure. In 2027, we will continue working on every front in banking financing and opportunities that may rise through the capital market.

I hope with this, I have answered your question. Thank you.

Operator: Thank you. Now we close the Q&A session, and we give way to Joaquín Gutiérrez, the CEO of Ecopetrol, for final remarks.

Joaquín Gutiérrez: Before I say goodbye, I would like to thank you for your time, for your questions, and above all, for your trust. Today, we have shown you the numbers, but I want to give you more. The certainty that we know where we are headed. We know our challenges, and we will not hide. We have the technical talent, the assets, and a clear plan to move from a horizon of reserves to one of decades, with discipline in each prize and a corporate governance that is among those of the best oil companies of the world. Again, I do not ask you to believe my words.

I ask you to look at us based on our results quarter by quarter, because that is the only language we will be talking. Ecopetrol will make it, and those that are with us today will be part of that story. Thank you. Because when Ecopetrol lifts, Colombia lifts. Welcome to the rebirth of Ecopetrol. Have a good day.

Operator: With this, we conclude our earnings call of the third quarter 2026. Thank you all for joining us. You may disconnect.

BYD’s new flagship Tang SUV starts at about $34,000 with two enormous screens

📅 Nov 13, 2026  |  Category: Technology  |  Read Source →

BYD opened pre-orders for the third-generation Tang starting at about $34,000 in China. This five-seat flagship SUV packs more than you’d expect for the money, with two massive 30-inch-plus screens inside and up to 850 km (528 miles) of CLTC range.

The third-generation Tang joins the Great Tang, or Da Tang, as it’s called in China in BYD’s expanding flagship SUV lineup.

We knew it was coming soon after the new model appeared in a regulatory filing in China’s Ministry of Industry and Information Technology (MIIT) last month.

BYD announced on Friday that pre-orders for the third-gen Tang are officially open, starting at 225,000 yuan, which is around $34,000.

The “large five-seat flagship SUV,” as BYD calls it, is available in three variants, priced from 225,000 yuan ($34,000), 240,000 yuan ($36,000), and 260,000 yuan ($39,000) with rear- and all-wheel-drive variants.

The two lower-priced variants are both RWD, offering a CLTC range of up to 850 km (528 miles), while the top-spec dual-motor AWD version has up to 770 km (478 miles).

All versions use BYD’s second-generation Blade Batteries and Flash Charging tech, enabling the battery to be charged from 10% to 80% in five minutes, or nine minutes to go from 10% to 97%.

Inside, the five-seat SUV packs a surprising amount of tech and premium extras for the price. The setup features two massive screens: a 35.4″ integrated display and another 30″ panoramic theater screen.

Unlike its predessor, which offered five and six-seat configurations, the third-gen Tang will only be offered as a five-seater.

The five-seat setup opens up interior space for passengers and the third-gen Tang has the largest front trunk (frunk) in its class at 205L, BYD said.

Like many of its new flagship vehicles, the third-gen Tang is equipped with BYD’s latest tech, including its God’s Eye B driver-assistance system and Disus-A dual-chamber intelligent air suspension body control system.

Other added features include a 22-speaker Dynaudio sound system, intelligent electric all‑wheel drive, a high-speed tire blowout stability system, and its iTAC intelligent torque control system.

Lu Tian, the general manager of BYD’s Dynasty division said third-gen Tang vehicles have arrived at dealerships nationwide.

The Great Tang received over 150,000 preorders by the time it launched in mid-June, a record for a single BYD model.

Since its official launch, the larger Great Tang (Da Tang) has sold more than 10,000 units for four straight months now.

The Great Tang is on sale in China in pure electric (EV) and plug-in hybrid (PHEV) powertrains. The cheapest EV variant starts at 239,900 yuan ($35,500) a CLTC driving range of 800 km (497 miles). Starting at just 269,900 yuan ($40,000), the Flagship EV version uses a bigger (130.15 kWh) battery, offering a CLTC range of up to 590 miles (950 km).

Will BYD’s smaller five-seat SUV draw the same demand? Let us know your thoughts in the comments below.

Dribblava i cani, rifiutò lo United per pioggia e niente patente: 10 cose assurde su Ronaldinho

📅 Nov 13, 2026  |  Category: Technology  |  Read Source →

Domenica 15 novembre, alle 17.30, il calcio riabbraccerà uno dei suoi interpreti più amati di sempre. Ronaldinho, a 46 anni, tornerà a disputare una partita ufficiale con la maglia del Ravenna, nel derby di Serie C contro il Forlì. Sono trascorsi oltre undici anni dalla sua ultima apparizione con la maglia del Fluminense, nel 2015. Un ritorno sorprendente, che ha riportato sotto i riflettori il fuoriclasse brasiliano, Pallone d'Oro nel 2005 e campione del mondo nel 2002. Ma dietro il sorriso, i dribbling e le giocate che hanno incantato il mondo si nascondono anche storie e aneddoti decisamente particolari. Ve ne abbiamo selezionate dieci.

No, non è uno scherzo. Ronaldinho ha raccontato di aver affinato la propria tecnica da bambino giocando con i suoi cani, Bala e Bombom. Una sorta di allenamento casalingo decisamente insolito: lui cercava di superarli con il pallone, mentre gli animali si divertivano a rincorrerlo. Quell'abitudine, a quanto pare, contribuì a sviluppare la sua straordinaria capacità di dribbling diventata il suo marchio di fabbrica.

Il soprannome Ronaldinho, letteralmente "piccolo Ronaldo", ha una storia particolare. In Brasile, prima dell'esplosione del Gaucho, il nome Ronaldo era già associato a un altro fenomeno: il centravanti Ronaldo Nazário, futuro Fenomeno. Per distinguere i due, il giovane talento venne identificato come Ronaldinho Gaúcho, con il secondo appellativo a richiamare la sua provenienza dal Rio Grande do Sul. Con il passare degli anni, il soprannome è rimasto legato soprattutto a lui, mentre l'ex Inter e Real Madrid è diventato universalmente conosciuto come Ronaldo il Fenomeno.

Durante i Mondiali del 2006, una modella francese di nome Alexandra Paressant attirò l'attenzione dei giornali di gossip sostenendo di avere una relazione con Ronaldinho. Raccontò addirittura di incontrarlo di nascosto durante il ritiro della Seleção e di trascorrere con lui notti di passione. La storia fece rapidamente il giro del mondo e venne associata anche alle prestazioni deludenti del Brasile in Germania. C'era però un problema: Ronaldinho smentì tutto e portò la donna in tribunale. Nel 2011 la modella venne condannata a risarcirlo per danni morali: il tribunale ritenne false le sue dichiarazioni.

Nonostante abbia collezionato automobili di lusso e guadagni milionari, Ronaldinho non ha mai conseguito la patente. Il brasiliano ha sempre manifestato una certa paura di mettersi al volante e, durante la carriera, ha preferito spostarsi in taxi oppure affidarsi al cugino Thiago, che si occupava dei suoi trasferimenti. Una particolarità decisamente curiosa per un campione abituato a correre a tutta velocità sul rettangolo verde.

Nel 2003 Ronaldinho è stato vicinissimo a vestire la maglia del Manchester United. Alla fine, però, il brasiliano scelse il Barcellona, dove avrebbe scritto alcune delle pagine più importanti della propria carriera. Sulla trattativa è rimasto un aneddoto raccontato da Quinton Fortune, ex centrocampista dei Red Devils: secondo lui, il maltempo avrebbe contribuito a far saltare l'operazione. "Penso che abbiamo sbagliato a portare Ronaldinho a Manchester nel periodo sbagliato dell'anno, perché quel giorno pioveva quando è arrivato all'aeroporto", disse Fortune.

Una curiosità che ha inevitabilmente attirato l'attenzione della cronaca italiana. Durante il processo Ruby emerse la testimonianza di Imane Fadil, che raccontò di una serata ad Arcore nella quale Iris Berardi si era presentata con una maschera raffigurante il volto di Ronaldinho e una maglia del Milan, prima di partecipare agli spettacoli organizzati durante la festa. Una precisazione è necessaria: il calciatore non era presente, ma venne utilizzata soltanto la sua immagine come travestimento.

Tra gli aneddoti più divertenti circolati negli anni c'è quello delle telefonate notturne di Ronaldinho ai compagni del Barcellona, alla vigilia di un Clásico contro il Real Madrid. Secondo la storia, il brasiliano avrebbe chiamato tutti annunciando un imminente trasferimento ai Blancos, chiedendo loro di mantenere il segreto. Prima della partita avrebbe poi svelato lo scherzo, trasformandolo in un discorso motivazionale per compattare lo spogliatoio. Una storia spesso attribuita all'autobiografia di Andrés Iniesta, ma smentita dallo stesso Ronaldinho in un'intervista a FourFourTwo: il racconto non era presente nel libro e non corrispondeva a un episodio reale. Il brasiliano ha comunque ammesso di essere sempre stato un grande amante degli scherzi ai compagni.

Il legame di Ronaldinho con la Spagna non si è limitato agli anni trascorsi al Barcellona. Il 27 agosto 2007 il brasiliano ha ottenuto ufficialmente la cittadinanza spagnola, dopo quattro anni di permanenza nel Paese. Ma c'è un altro particolare curioso: nell'agosto 2006 era diventato socio onorario del Burela Fútbol Sala, squadra galiziana di calcio a cinque. Un riconoscimento ricevuto quando era ancora una delle principali stelle del Barcellona e del calcio mondiale.

Non soltanto calcio. Nell'aprile 2011 Ronaldinho ricevette la medaglia Machado de Assis, la più alta onorificenza dell'Academia Brasileira de Letras. Un riconoscimento insolito per un calciatore, tanto che durante la cerimonia venne presentato scherzosamente come "Doutor Ronaldinho". Non si trattava, però, di una laurea honoris causa, come spesso riportato. Qualche anno più tardi si è anche cimentato con il cinema: nel 2018 è apparso in "Kickboxer: Retaliation", film d'azione con Jean-Claude Van Damme e Mike Tyson.

La parentesi più difficile della sua vita lontano dal campo risale al marzo 2020. Ronaldinho venne arrestato in Paraguay insieme al fratello Roberto Assis dopo essere entrato nel Paese con documenti e passaporti falsi. Inizialmente finì in un carcere di Asunción, dove rimase per circa un mese, prima di essere trasferito agli arresti domiciliari in un hotel di lusso. La vicenda si concluse nell'agosto dello stesso anno, dopo oltre cinque mesi di detenzione complessiva: un giudice dispose una condanna a due anni con sospensione della pena e una multa di 200.000 dollari da pagare insieme al fratello. Non si trattò dunque di due distinti arresti, ma di un'unica vicenda giudiziaria che tenne il campione brasiliano lontano dalla libertà per mesi.

Sotheby’s Hedreen Auction Proceeds Will Repay Loan, Not Fund University Museum: Report

📅 Nov 13, 2026  |  Category: Technology  |  Read Source →

A $300 million art donation to Seattle University has been revealed to be more complex than originally reported.

On Monday, Sotheby’s will begin selling approximately 50 works from the collection amassed by Seattle real estate developer and philanthropist Richard “Dick” Hedreen, previously billed as the “largest art gift ever” to a US university. Hedreen said in September that the sale did not mean the promised gift was falling through. Rather, he suggested, the proceeds would help furnish Seattle University’s new art museum, where the collection was eventually expected to hang. Related Articles As Art Week NYC Kicks Off, Galleries Have Millions of Dollars of Art to Sell--And No Shortage of Naras Paris Court Orders Sotheby's to Return Nazi-Looted Pissarro Painting to Jewish Heirs

Records newly reported on by The Seattle Times, however, tell a different story: The works are being sold not to fund the museum, but to pay off a debt secured against the collection — a debt Hedreen says stands between him and fulfilling his pledge to the university.

What was originally framed as a donation, in reality, was a pledge: Hedreen, whose firm R.C. Hedreen Co. owns the Grand Hyatt among other Seattle properties, retained the right to buy and sell works from the collection until the gift was finalized. The Sotheby’s auction is expected to generate at least $100 million across a series of sales. As of writing, the overall value of the artwork set for Seattle University is unclear.

The sale spans an expansive sweep of art history. Works by Willem de Kooning, Rashid Johnson, Amy Sherald, and Jonas Wood will be offered alongside Old Masters by Bronzino, Jacopo Pontormo, Élisabeth Vigée Le Brun, and Thomas Gainsborough. Leading Sotheby’s offerings is Roy Lichtenstein’s Reflections on “Painter and Model” (1990), estimated at $20 million to $30 million. Cecily Brown’s 12-foot-wide Untitled (Shipwreck) (2017), estimated at $7 million to $10 million, is another highlight.

A news release from Seattle University stated: “Hedreen has given the university the renowned Hedreen Collection (named for himself and his late wife Elizabeth ‘Betty’ Ann Petri Hedreen, a Seattle University alumna) valued at $300 million, along with $25 million in seed funding to develop the museum.”

However, the Seattle Times reported that records show that the “seed funding” was actually provided through the private sale of a de Kooning painting. Estimated in 2024 to be worth $25 million, the work fetched $17 million.

The Sotheby’s sale proceeds will instead go toward paying off an outstanding loan secured against the collection. The borrowing dates to 2012, when Hedreen first pledged several artworks, including a pieces by Francis Bacon, de Kooning and David Hockney, as collateral for a Goldman Sachs loan. In 2025, that financing was replaced with a Bank of America loan secured by approximately 44 major artworks.

R.C. Hedreen Co. President David Thyer confirmed that the Sotheby’s sale is tied to financial pressures in the hotel and real estate markets. Income generated by the company’s properties, he said, is insufficient to repay the loan “in any reasonably foreseeable time frame.”

ARTnews has contacted Sotheby’s for comment.

Richard Hedreen’s son, Guy, said in a statement that his family’s “commitment to Seattle University has not changed.” His father, Guy added, “is not selling works from his collection in order to spend money on himself” or his business. Rather, the auction reflects his commitment to the university “by removing a debt that prevents him from translating his pledge intentions into an outright gift.”

Construction on the new art museum, originally scheduled to begin this past summer, has been pushed to 2027 as the project awaits permits. Designed by local architect Tom Kundig, the museum is intended to permanently house the Hedreen collection, alongside rotating exhibitions and academic programming.

Scott K. McClellan, Seattle University’s vice president for university affairs, said the school continues to believe the collection “presents a significant opportunity for the university, community and our broader vision for the arts.” He reiterated the “shared intention of Mr. Hedreen and the university” that Seattle University ultimately receive a collection of museum-quality works commensurate with the collection that formed the basis of the original commitment.

McClellan added that it had been “reasonable” for the university to expect Hedreen’s loan to be paid off by 2028, when the museum was originally scheduled for completion.

Should Government Officials Be Banned From Prediction Markets? House Oversight Says Maybe

📅 Nov 13, 2026  |  Category: Technology  |  Read Source →

Should Government Officials Be Banned From Prediction Markets? House Oversight Says Maybe

The chairman of the House Oversight Committee is raising the prospect of barring government officials from trading on prediction markets, citing concerns that insiders are using nonpublic information to profit on platforms like Kalshi and Polymarket.

In a staff memorandum released Friday, the committee detailed an investigation into insider trading by government officials, military personnel and people holding security clearances who have placed bets using information not available to the public.

The probe gathered nearly 1,700 pages of documents and included five bipartisan staff briefings from prediction market platforms about how they detect and report suspicious activity.

Chairman James Comer framed the issue around public trust. “Americans are rightly concerned about public corruption,” he said, arguing Congress “has a duty to ensure our laws adequately prevent insider trading” and prohibit officials “from profiting from nonpublic information.”

He said lawmakers must weigh “additional reforms to prevent public corruption and restore the American people’s trust in government.”

The memo’s central recommendation is that Congress consider new restrictions on federal personnel, ranging from an outright ban on trading prediction markets to narrower limits on specific contracts, such as those tied to government decisions.

Applying such rules across all branches, the committee argued, would reinforce that government employees shouldn’t financially benefit from access to nonpublic information, and would remove even the appearance of it.

The memo stressed that existing insider trading laws already apply to prediction markets. It cited the Commodity Exchange Act, as amended by the STOCK Act, which bars federal employees, members of Congress and others from trading commodity futures, options, or swaps on nonpublic information drawn from their positions.

The committee credited the platforms themselves for policing their markets, noting that most reported insider trading incidents were first detected and flagged to regulators by the platforms. It singled out Polymarket, which recently hired a former law enforcement expert and rolled out surveillance software called “PolyGraph” built with analytics firm Chainalysis, and Kalshi for strengthening their detection protocols.

The scrutiny follows a string of cases involving alleged insider trading by public officials and government employees.

A U.S. soldier was charged in April over alleged Polymarket trades tied to a military operation that resulted in the capture of Venezuelan President Nicolas Maduro. Then, in August, the CFTC fined a former White House teleprompter operator for betting on presidential speeches.

The push also lands amid broader pressure on the sector, with the CFTC moving to assert authority over prediction markets in its fight against several states, while Sen. Elizabeth Warren sets her sights on the industry as the incoming Senate Banking chair.

Jensen Huang Steps Down as CEO. Is Nvidia Still a Buy?

📅 Nov 13, 2026  |  Category: Technology  |  Read Source →

A slew of extramarital affairs and security risks have shaken Nvidia's investment thesis to the core.

Nvidia (NVDA -2.01%) has been tethered to the spotlight since Nov. 8, when founder and then-CEO Jensen Huang admitted to having an affair with Alice Huang.

Alice Huang (no relation to Jensen) was an executive at a Singapore-based Nvidia customer. And reports indicate that U.S. officials and the FBI are investigating the relationship because Alice Huang is suspected of being a Chinese intelligence operative. On Nov. 10, Nvidia hired a prominent New York law firm to initiate an independent investigation into the affair.

The situation escalated after market close on Nov. 11, when the New York Times reported that Jensen Huang had a romantic relationship with Nvidia's vice president of healthcare, Kimberly Powell. On Nov. 12, reports surfaced of a possible third affair with a second employee. That same day, Nvidia published a press release, announcing that Huang had resigned, with CFO Colette Kress taking over as interim CEO.

As of market close on Nov. 12, Nvidia is down a staggering 27.5% in the last month. Here's how the recent news surrounding Huang impacts Nvidia's investment thesis and whether the growth stock is still a buy.

Jensen Huang was arguably the most important CEO in the world. He co-founded Nvidia with Chris Malachowsky and Curtis Priem 33 years ago and possesses a rare balance of technical, strategic, marketing, and leadership skills.

I recently read The Nvidia Way: Jensen Huang and the Making of a Tech Giant by Tae Kim. It's an excellent book that serves as both a history of Nvidia and a biography of Huang. And the biggest takeaway I had from the book is that Huang is arguably more integral to Nvidia than Steve Jobs was to Apple (AAPL +0.39%), Bill Gates was to Microsoft, or Jeff Bezos was to Amazon.

Tim Cook was able to catapult Apple's market cap up severalfold, even after Jobs's passing, by leveraging Apple's integrated ecosystem of products and services. Nvidia is different. When it comes to artificial intelligence (AI) in the semiconductor industry, competition is fierce, and innovation is paramount. Huang has made several key decisions throughout the company's history. And without them, it's highly unlikely the tech stock would be where it is today.

Huang was a firm believer in parallel computing and the graphics processing unit (GPU) -- a term Nvidia helped coin when it debuted the GeForce 256 in 1999 -- and labeled it the world's first GPU. He was also adamant about CUDA, which is Nvidia's software architecture used by developers with Nvidia GPUs.

By integrating hardware and software, Nvidia paved a runway for repeat business in an industry where a hot product can leapfrog the moats of even the most entrenched industry leaders. After all, Huang saw this in the early days of Nvidia, when the company almost went bankrupt multiple times because it developed products too infrequently and bet the farm on a single launch.

Huang also has an impeccable work ethic and top-to-bottom devotion to Nvidia -- arguably to a fault. Nvidia's organization structure positioned Huang like Jupiter, surrounded by dozens of direct reports that orbit him like moons. This wasn’t just so Huang could keep tabs. It's so that he can contribute through spontaneous bursts of creativity.

To complement this workflow, Nvidia has whiteboards scattered throughout meeting rooms for ideation and real-time problem-solving. The knowledge and willingness to break concepts down, start from scratch, and hold frequent open discussions have become essential parts of Nvidia's corporate culture. And it remains to be seen if that will persist under new leadership.

Huang has also been a firm proponent of AI. And it's a key reason why Nvidia was able to shift its end markets from gaming, professional visualization, and automotive to data centers in just a few years. Gaining a foothold in AI was one thing. But Nvidia has continued to push the bounds of innovation through incredibly impressive product upgrades.

Nvidia could have easily taken its foot off the gas after announcing its Grace Blackwell architecture in March 2024. Blackwell unlocked massive efficiency improvements and cost reductions in AI training. But Nvidia really outdid itself with Vera Rubin, which it announced in January 2026.

Rubin's full-stack platform includes six chips, allowing Nvidia to control a larger share of data center infrastructure. Rubin's cost reductions and efficiency improvements compared to Blackwell are so massive that hyperscalers are lining up at the door to place orders. On the Aug. 26 Q2 fiscal 2027 earnings call, Nvidia said that it began shipping Rubin in August and expects it to account for 20% of its Q3 data center revenue, which it will report on Nov. 17.

Reports of three affairs, an internal investigation, an FBI probe, and now Jensen Huang resigning as CEO directly impact the investment thesis. Until we get answers on the extent of the damage and the next long-term CEO, the stock simply isn't as strong a buy as it used to be -- even with a valuation that's ridiculously cheap for such a high-quality business.

I wouldn't sell Nvidia simply because the company's operating leverage and market position are impossible to ignore. But it's hard to imagine Nvidia without Jensen Huang's leadership. The situation would be different if Huang had a clear successor, but there's nothing of the kind at Nvidia, which poses a major key-person risk.

At the very least, Nvidia must overhaul its organizational structure. Poor character aside, I'd expect any replacement to fall short of Huang's caliber as a CEO. And that's legitimately concerning for investors.

As tempting as the sell-off looks at first glance, investors are better off waiting for the dust to settle. I'd much rather buy the stock at a higher price with greater clarity than bottom-fish in waters this murky.

Nakamoto Sells More Bitcoin (BTC)

📅 2026-11-13  |  Category: Technology  |  Read Source →

David Bailey's Bitcoin treasury company, Nakamoto Inc. (NASDAQ: NAKA), has reduced its Bitcoin (BTC) holdings by another 184 BTC, according to its latest quarterly financial results.

The company, which has made Bitcoin central to its corporate strategy, held 4,283 BTC as of Sept. 30, down from 4,467 BTC at the end of June.

According to Bitcoin Treasuries, Nakamoto sold 184 BTC during the third quarter of 2026, bringing its total holdings to 4,283 BTC. The company currently ranks 21st on the Bitcoin 100 corporate treasury ranking.

The latest financial filing shows that Nakamoto generated approximately $13.2 million in additional proceeds from digital asset sales during the quarter. However, the filing does not separately identify the 184 BTC reduction as a single sale.

Despite the reduction in its holdings, Nakamoto's remaining Bitcoin was valued at approximately $357.9 million at the end of September.

This is not the first time Bailey's company has reduced its Bitcoin holdings in 2026.

In March, Nakamoto sold approximately 284 BTC for $20 million to support working capital requirements and fund operations following its acquisitions of BTC Inc. and UTXO Management.

The sale took place at an average price of roughly $70,422 per Bitcoin, significantly below the company's original acquisition cost.

During the first quarter, Nakamoto also received approximately 43 BTC in premiums from its derivatives strategies before selling around 40 BTC.

The company made another substantial Bitcoin sale in June.

According to its second-quarter financial results, Nakamoto sold approximately 600 BTC for 35.6 million USDT.

Together with proceeds from certain Bitcoin derivative positions, the transactions generated approximately $48 million.

Nakamoto used $45 million of those proceeds to partially repay its Bitcoin-backed loan from cryptocurrency exchange Kraken, reducing the outstanding principal from 210 million USDT to 165 million USDT.

The latest reduction in Bitcoin holdings comes as Nakamoto continues to manage its substantial debt obligations while expanding its operating businesses.

As of Sept. 30, approximately 3,030 BTC were pledged as collateral against the company's debt, leaving 1,253 BTC unencumbered.

On Nov. 11, Nakamoto extended the maturity of 60 million USDT of its Kraken loan from December 2026 to December 2027. The company now has no scheduled principal repayments remaining in 2026.

Interestingly, Nakamoto reported a net profit of $85.5 million for the third quarter, primarily benefiting from a substantial increase in the market value of its Bitcoin holdings.

However, its adjusted operating loss reached $15.8 million, highlighting the ongoing challenge of generating sustainable earnings beyond Bitcoin price appreciation.

Bailey has maintained that Nakamoto intends to build businesses capable of generating revenue throughout different Bitcoin market cycles.

The company's latest Bitcoin holdings reduction illustrates how corporate treasury strategies can involve selling cryptocurrency to meet financing and operational requirements, rather than simply accumulating BTC indefinitely.

Networks scrambled find the reasons behind on a highly unusual nationwide ground stop ordered by the FAA on Friday due to “extraordinary circumstances” and “reasons of safety.”

Secretary of Transportation Sean Duffy said that the order followed “a communication issue impacting all Air Route Control Centers. FAA technicians are investigating the issue.”

The FAA ordered all departing traffic stopped “until further notice” and inbound international flights would be managed “on a case by case basis.”

Fox News reported on the ground stop, followed by CNN and MS NOW. Broadcast networks also broke into regular programming with special reports.

There was a ground stop in 2023 that lasted for about an hour and a half, reportedly due to a breakdown in a pilot alert system. The FAA also issued a ground stop following the terrorist attacks on Sept. 11, 2001.

Before or After the Snow? Here’s The Right Time to Salt Your Driveway This Winter

📅 Nov 13, 2026  |  Category: Technology  |  Read Source →

Salt your driveway like a pro this winter with these expert-backed tips.

The Centers for Disease Control report a 35 percent increase in outdoor slips and falls during the winter months. Icy sidewalks, stairs and driveways are largely to blame for this seasonal uptick. Icy driveways are not only dangerous to walk on but also make driving and parking difficult. Ice melt salt is one of the best ways to keep driveways clear, but how should you best use it? Should you apply it before or after a storm — or both? Two industry experts weigh in on the best way to salt your driveway for optimal results this winter.

It depends. Salting before a big storm with heavy snow and ice accumulation is a good idea, but timing matters.

Plan to apply salt several hours before a storm hits for best results. It creates a salty coating that prevents the initial snowfall from bonding with the driveway surface. Pre-salting reduces total snow accumulation and makes snow removal easier once the storm is over. Avoid doing it too far in advance, or if rain is in the forecast, as the salt can be washed or blown away before the snow arrives. It’s not necessary to salt before a light snowfall of less than six inches.

It’s always a good idea to salt after it snows, but don’t rely on it to replace snow removal efforts. First remove as much snow as possible with a shovel or snowblower, then use salt to clear away any remaining slush and snow.

The best time to salt is immediately after snow removal, before cold nighttime temperatures set in. “When applied before the temperatures drop, salt prevents ice from forming and ensures the snow does not freeze solid to the ground,” Angelika Klimorowski, a winter maintenance specialist at Online Rock Salt, says.

Apply salt before and after a big storm for best results, especially when heavy snow, sleet or freezing rain is expected.

“Pre-salting helps prevent ice from bonding to the driveway, while a second application can help break down any ice that forms,” Aaron Strickland, owner of Mr. Trim Lawn & Garden Services, says. “For a typical snowfall, both applications usually aren’t necessary.”

If only a few inches of snow is forecast, salting after snow removal is likely sufficient. Applying salt before and after a small storm is not only a waste of money, but can damage driveway surfaces, burn lawn edges and generate harmful storm runoff.

“The exact amount of rock salt that you need will depend on how big the area is that you are planning to de-ice,” Klimorowski says. A little goes a long way, and more is not better. A 12-oz. coffee mug filled with salt is enough to treat a 1,000-sq.-ft. driveway. Spread salt evenly, leaving about 3 inches between salt grains. Avoid overapplication, as it can degrade driveway surfaces and harm the environment.

A handheld spreader makes application easier and more consistent, especially for larger driveways. “We try to follow recommended coverage rates and use a spreader wherever possible,” Strickland says. “Too much salt wastes product and can damage surrounding grass and gardens.” If spreading salt by hand, always wear gloves and cascade it for even coverage.

Rock salt is only effective down to ground temperatures of 15 to 17 degrees. Don’t apply more salt if it’s not working, as it’s likely too cold out for it to work. “If your area tends to experience colder temperatures, you may need to find an alternative de-icing product to keep your surfaces safe,” Klimorowski says. Calcium chloride and magnesium chloride ice melt products are two common alternatives that remain effective at sub-zero temperatures.

Yes, rain will wash away salt rather than dissolve it. It then ends up in storm drains and potentially pollutes local bodies of water.

Rock salt stops working in temperatures below 10 to 15 degrees.

“While it is true that salt will work best if you apply it before the temperatures drop, there is no deadline, and you can always apply it later,” Klimorowski says.

There’s no guarantee salt will damage your driveway, but you should use it sparingly. Concrete driveways are particularly vulnerable to salt damage.

6 Most-Anticipated Steam Games You Should Add to Your Wishlist Right Now

📅 Nov 13, 2026  |  Category: Technology  |  Read Source →

Sometimes when we put together a new installment of Wishlister, Polygon’s weekly newsletter that highlights upcoming Steam games, we look to events like Steam Next Fest and the Triple-I Initiative to dig up gems. But the most tried-and-true method we’ve found for finding promising games is simply stumbling into them by accident.

A weekly cheat sheet to what you’ll eventually love to play. No buying, no commitment — just saving for later.

All of the games in this week’s Wishlister follow that methodology. These are six games that flew onto our radar without looking for them, whether we bumped into their demos at live events or had them recommended to us by friends. We’re paying that energy forward this week, as you’ll see in this grab bag of upcoming PC games that deserve a spot on your Steam wishlist.

With Bub, developer Paperfrog looks well on its way to delivering a jaw-dropping debut. The narrative game takes anxiety around a developer’s real-life Multiple Sclerosis diagnosis and channels it into a personal story about preserving your memory in art. That’s reinforced by an analog art style that looks downright incredible.

False Echo turns wartime cryptography into a life-or-death narrative game. You play as a code cracker aboard a submarine in 1942, and you’re tasked with processing incoming transmissions. There are some clear shades of Papers, Please here, which should make for a brainy political thriller steeped in World War II history.

If you’re looking for your next high-score chasing game after this month’s great Faraway, Strikemania is one to watch. The retro arcade game is basically a reverse Missile Command; you control the missile attacking enemy defenses. With slick 2D art that evokes Geometry Wars, it should be a direct hit for fans of retro games.

Floor Plan: Slap It Out! hinges on a satisfyingly goofy gameplay hook: You can slap the crap out of everything. The latest game from developer Turbo Button is a physical comedy that has you solving puzzles using your noodly arms. It’s also the developer’s first non-VR game, so you’ll be able to enjoy the studio’s quirky brand of humor without a headset.

A perfect compliment to this year’s excellent Over the Hill, Hodka is an open-world driving game that has you fixing up an old RV. You’ll use a metal detector to dig up scrap that can be used to upgrade your van. It also seems to have a spooky undertone to it, making it look like an on-land alternative to Dredge.

For those who love RPGs that give you a lot of freedom, World of Anterra seems poised to become a hidden gem. The retro-inspired game drops you in a pixel art world and sets you loose to discover its non-linear story. It’s all meticulously hand-crafted to give players a retro RPG that’s as rich with secrets as an Elder Scrolls game. (And it will likely be out before Sentinel!)

With all of the new titles regularly popping up on Netflix each month, it can be hard to narrow down exactly what you want to watch once you've plopped yourself on the couch and picked up the old remote. But with yet another weekend upon us, we at Tom's Guide thought we'd make things easier for you and curate an easy, breezy list of three movies that are well worth your time and attention. If you're a horror-movie fan, you'll be happy to hear that the freaky 2022 hit "Smile" is back on the streaming service. For action lovers, there's the new Indonesian survival thriller, but if country music and Jessica Chastain dramas are more your vibe, the streamer's new Nashville-set mystery original might be up your alley.

That's enough to keep you busy until Monday, surely. Here are three new-to-Netflix movies to add to your weekend watchlist.

Jessica Chastain has famously played a country star before — she received both Emmy and Golden Globe nods for her work as the heavily hair-sprayed crooner Tammy Wynette in "George & Tammy" — but "Heartland" pulls that musical beat into the mystery genre.

In this Nashville-set noir written and directed by Shana Feste ("Country Strong", "The Greatest"), the Oscar winner plays former country singer Misty Jones, who comes out of retirement to investigate the disappearance of her niece (Carter Faith), who is also a rising country performer. Misty's search forces her to grapple not only with Nashville's seedy underbelly but also the demons of her own past.

When a gang of ruthless criminals descends on a small remote village in Central Java, guilt-ridden grandfather Eyang Hari (Lukman Sardi) becomes an unlikely hero, tapping into his past as a former special forces soldier to save the lives of his two estranged grandchildren, Putri (Callista Arum) and Dimas (Sultan Hamonangan).

Directed by Sidharta Tata, the action film “balances high-stakes combat with a heartfelt story about family,” says Netflix, “highlighting the emotional journey of three generations as they rebuild trust amid grief and atone for the past.”

After witnessing a brutal and bizarre incident involving a patient, Dr. Rose Cotter’s (Sosie Bacon) life begins to unravel, with terrifying visions and disturbing occurrences disrupting her days.

Those increasingly sinister and supernatural events all seem tied to a malevolent entity that feeds on trauma and passes from victim to witness, each marked by an unsettling fixed grin. To break the deadly curse before it destroys her, Rose turns to an old flame (Kyle Gallner) for help and must confront her troubled past to escape her horrifying new reality.

Written and directed by Parker Finn in his feature directorial debut, “Smile” was an absolute horror hit, grossing $217.40 million worldwide and winning over critics. With an 80% approval rating on Rotten Tomatoes, the site’s critical consensus praised: "Deeply creepy visuals and a standout Sosie Bacon further elevate Smile's unsettling exploration of trauma, adding up to the rare feature that satisfyingly expands on a short."

I Just Found Out Tom Holland Asked To Tailor His Odyssey Skirt Shorter For A Hilarious Reason

📅 Nov 13, 2026  |  Category: Technology  |  Read Source →

It's Really Trippy Watching Tom Holland Pop In And Out Of His Peter Parker Spider-Man Accent

Unlock instant access to exclusive member features.

Get full access to premium articles, exclusive features and a growing list of member rewards.

You can’t slip anything by Christopher Nolan.

When you purchase through links on our site, we may earn an affiliate commission. Here’s how it works.

The Odyssey, one of the highest-grossing films of the 2026 movie schedule, is a serious movie that blends mythological themes and modern storytelling as it explores the journey of Odysseus as he faces a dangerous voyage through fantastical realms on his way back to his family. And its director, Christopher Nolan, is a revered filmmaker known for paying attention to little details as he brings his signature narrative and visual style to life on the big screen. That doesn’t mean hilarious behind-the-scenes moments and shenanigans don’t still happen during production, and one in particular involves Tom Holland requesting skirt alterations.

The fantasy epic marked Tom Holland’s first time working with the filmmaker, and boy does he have some memorable stories to share from the experience. For example, according to Variety, who was at the November 10 FYC event, the current Spider-Man decided to take matters of his skirt into his own hands with costume designer Ellen Mirojnick, asking her to hem it to help give him some height on screen.

In the 75-minute documentary Behind the Scenes of The Odyssey, chronicling the making of the three-hour blockbuster, Holland revealed that his reasoning for the change didn’t matter because you can’t sneak anything by Christopher Nolan. He said:

I asked Ellen, the costume designer, I said, ‘Could you just make the skirt a little shorter?’ It’ll make me look a little taller.

Estimated to fall somewhere between 5’6” and 5’8”, I can absolutely understand wanting to appear as tall as possible while starring in such a big movie.

I also appreciate that the Peter Parker actor has enough self-awareness to make such a request. Apparently, he even got the request fulfilled when the costume designer had his tailored skirt ready six weeks later. However, Christopher Nolan noticed that something was amiss and had the following question:

Two seconds into taking in the costume, [Christopher Nolan] was like, ‘How come the skirt is shorter?’

Critics heaped praise on The Odyssey, and the box office numbers reflected that audiences couldn’t get enough, so the length of Tom Holland’s skirt clearly had no effect on the ultimate outcome. But personally, I now wish I knew what the actor’s response was when the Inception director asked what happened. Did he cop to his attempt to look taller on screen? Did he try to play it off in some other way? Did the filmmaker find it as hilarious as I do?

Without a camera turned on them both in that moment, we might never know for sure, but I’m not surprised that someone as meticulous as Christopher Nolan noticed almost immediately and called out the change.

There have already been plenty of memorable stories about the shoot, as Matt Damon talked about being “wet” and “cold” during portions of the production while Charlize Theron acted through massive winds that got up to 30 to 40 miles per hour. Now, I hope we get more silly tales like this one from Tom Holland.

With The Odyssey expected to be a major awards contender this year, I feel like that's very possible. So, here's hoping we get to learn more about Tom Holland trying to shorten his skirt for this Christopher Nolan epic.
